185. 2026-08-27 · **Upload-and-analyze ships** — the post-MVP queue item, pulled forward on Timothy's call · any audio file through the unchanged engine, banked as lesson `upload`, so real-stakes speech (a meeting, a memo) gets the same mirror as a practice run. No envelope exists for a file and the engine already treats absent evidence honestly (#122). Two open dials, recorded not hidden: an uploaded file banks as TODAY's speaking (streak and coin), which is right for fresh audio and generous for old files; and the star cut points were tuned on 60–90s takes, so long files read the per-minute rates fine but stars loosely. Revisit both if uploads become a daily habit rather than a mirror

186. 2026-08-27 · **Real reminders: web push, server-sent** — Timothy's call ("we need true reminders as well and notifications") · the browser-scheduled tiers only ever rang on Chromium; a push subscription rings everywhere permission is granted, app closed, iPhone included once installed to the home screen. Shape: `push_subscriptions` (migration 0008) carries hour + UTC offset + quiet hours per browser; an hourly tick (GitHub Actions, because Vercel Hobby crons are daily-only) hits `/api/push/cron`, which applies quiet hours, the #143 done-today rule against the reps table itself, and a one-send-per-local-day stamp — all pure and tested in `lib/push.ts`. The local tiers stay as fallback and go dark when push holds, so a day never double-rings. Needs ops once: VAPID keypair + CRON_SECRET in Vercel, CRON_SECRET in GitHub secrets

187. 2026-08-27 · **The Presence calibration bench** — Timothy's call ("truly finish body posture and confidence and body language") · /calibrate (off the nav, linked from settings) runs four labeled 20-second takes (composed / slouch / look-away / hands-hidden) through the REAL sampler and scorer, prints measured-vs-current, and proposes a constant set via `lib/presence-fit.ts` (pure, 9 tests): composed defines good with a margin, the adversarial takes bound bad, and anything the takes can't separate becomes a printed warning instead of an invented number. The download includes raw frames so candidate constants can be re-scored offline. What "finish" still needs and code can't provide: Timothy in front of the camera once — the bench makes that twenty minutes instead of a research project

188. 2026-08-27 · **The first real camera session broke three Presence assumptions, and each got a structural fix, not a threshold tweak** · Timothy ran the bench honestly (propped phone, real slouch, real 20s look-away) and the engine failed all three ways: a HELD slump out-scored the animated upright take (every posture signal measured wander, so motionless bad posture was invisible), a full look-away read as 100% eye contact (the yaw check compares nose to both ears, and turning far enough hides the far ear, switching the check off exactly when it mattered), and hands-in-pockets counted 77 "gestures" a minute (the tracker hallucinates plausible wrists and the counter believed 66ms of jitter). The fixes: posture is now the WORSE of wander and `headLift` (median nose-height above the shoulder line, a new persisted metric, nullable on old rows); eye-line gains a nose-between-the-eyes yaw proxy that sharpens as the head turns instead of going blind, halved tolerance when an ear is missing; gestures need 4 sustained frames inside a bounded speed band, and a wrist estimated outside the frame is unmeasurable, not slow. The bench fits the slump band from the composed-vs-slouch takes (the pair finally separates what he actually performed), and each failure carries a regression test named for this session

189. 2026-08-27 · **The second clean session: adopt what separated, unscore what didn't** · Timothy's redone propped takes fixed gestures (pockets 77→15/min, slouch 3) and drift (0.03–0.07, sane), so the posture and head wander bands adopt his fitted values (0.038/0.228, 0.088/0.469 — n=1, provisional, but the v1 guesses scored his ordinary head movement 18/100). Two signals did NOT earn a score: `headLift` swung with arm position and torso turn instead of slouch (shoulder-width is an unstable normalizer — pockets narrow it, gesturing widens it, turning foreshortens it), so slump goes measured-but-unscored, the exact #119 pattern, pending raw-frame analysis from the bench download; and the eye check reads HEAD pose only — Timothy's look-away was mostly eyes, not head, and pose landmarks cannot see pupils. Gaze needs MediaPipe's Face Landmarker (iris points, on-device, ~3MB model): queued as a real upgrade, not faked with thresholds. The eye dimension's claim stays honest as written ("head up and facing the camera")

190. 2026-08-27 · **Presence research pass: the four dimensions hold, the refusals get recorded, iris gaze is the one real upgrade** (docs/presence-research.md) · gesture rate is the best-validated visual correlate of speaking quality in the literature (r=.820 USC ICT), head-pose-as-eye-contact is the universal method that only we and VirtualSpeech admit to, and the one published cadence threshold (Presentation Trainer's 6s-without-a-gesture) supports our zone floor. Newly RECORDED refusals, with the HireVue precedent (facial analysis dropped Jan 2021, ~0.25% predictive power, bias findings): no facial-expression or smile scoring, no emotion/confidence inference from the face, no attire/background judgment. Queued, not adopted: MediaPipe iris gaze (the honest eyes-vs-head fix from #189), ETS bounding-volume openness as a slump-replacement candidate for the raw-frame analysis, and gesture-speech synchrony (unmeasured by anyone; we uniquely hold word timestamps and pose timelines in one product). Also market-validated: on-device, derived-numbers-only is the strongest privacy stance shipping anywhere and belongs in marketing copy

191. 2026-08-27 · **The neck gap: Timothy's slump candidate, measured in face-sized units** — his call ("for slouch maybe neck visibility is good") · the measurable version of "how much neck is showing": the mouth-to-shoulder-line gap in units of the speaker's own inter-eye distance. Face geometry is rigid, so this normalizer is immune to the exact failure that unscored headLift (#189: pockets narrow the shoulders, gesturing widens them, turning foreshortens them). Frontal frames only — a turned head foreshortens the eye gap and the measurement returns null rather than a wrong number. Pose capture gains the two mouth landmarks it needed. Same discipline as its rival: measured, persisted, on the bench beside lift, UNSCORED — the next calibration session decides which of the two separates a real slouch, and the winner takes the posture dimension's slump half

192. 2026-08-27 · **The neck gap wins the slump candidacy and gets SCORED; Presence is calibrated on a real body** · raw-frame analysis of Timothy's two bench downloads settled #191's head-to-head: on the committed-slouch session the neck gap dropped 47% at 3.7x the frame noise (composed 1.90 → slouch 1.00) where headLift managed 40% at 2.9x, and on the shallow-slouch control session NEITHER separated, which is the control behaving correctly. The same frames proved #189's diagnosis outright: his shoulder width swung 27% between takes from arm position alone. Adopted: NECK_GOOD 1.7 / NECK_BAD 1.05 (posture = worse of wander and slump, wander-only when no frontal face measured), gesture zone top 26 → 34 (the hardened detector read his genuinely animated speech at 26.9/min in both sessions; the ceiling was folklore, the floor has literature #190). headLift stays measured for cross-session comparability, retired from scoring. Also confirmed working on real frames: the profile-view eye fix (look-away 39–42% vs 94–100%) and the gesture gates (pockets 0–3/min, was 77). All n=1 and marked provisional; the bench refits in twenty minutes whenever a second body shows up

193. 2026-08-27 · **A hot mic announces itself with the live level, never with a bare countdown** · reported by Timothy on Hostile Q&A ("I didn't even know it was listening, I thought it was note taking or thinking time") — and the confusion was earned: this product HAS an opt-in think-time step (#35), so a countdown with no recording affordance legitimately reads as prep. The fix is measurement, not decoration: a dot and a bar driven by the actual mic RMS, which move when you speak and sit still when you don't — the one proof of listening a screen can give (and why the banned-tells rule against pulsing badges doesn't apply: this pulse IS data). Buttons say the verb outright: "Record my take", "Record my answer". Rule for any future recording surface: if the mic is hot, something on screen must move with the voice

194. 2026-08-27 · **The games dogfood batch, from Timothy's first real session on the paid tier** · five fixes, one root cause among them: (a) a game's staged mods no longer consult the entitlement — the resolver dropped premium-staged mods for free accounts (and during the profile-fetch race), which silently killed the Q&A interruption AND the game's identity, making it end like a lesson; premium gates the mod PICKER, never the games, and a stacked extra still respects it (tested). (b) The tight timer is 30 seconds, was 45 — not tight enough to force the compression it exists to train; Speed rush copy follows. (c) A game debrief ends as a game: Another round / Retake / Back to Tools, never a push onto the path's next lesson. (d) The hostile debrief shows the take's speech numbers (fillers, WPM, held pauses, Index) — it ran the full engine, so hiding its measurements was withholding the product. (e) Demos's sprite matches what he's doing: listening while you speak, the workout while he thinks (no thinking pose exists; the gym's idea of thinking), speaking when he asks. Plus the upload duration gate: scoring has 60s of wall time (Vercel Hobby), so files are gated at ~6 minutes BEFORE the upload spends, with the honest reason, and a 502/504 says "trim it" instead of "server didn't answer"
195. 2026-08-27 · **Data deltas: sage when the number moved the right way, terracotta when it moved the wrong way** — Timothy's call ("red for bad, green for good, more fillers in red") · direction-aware per metric (fillers falling is good, index falling is bad), applied to the home card's since-day-one line (which was BACKWARDS: a rising index printed terracotta), the results delta, the comparison card, the sparkline chip and endpoint dot, and the gains chips (a drop wore sage). ⚠ Flagged the way #88 was: this widens terracotta's meaning — "tap this" everywhere, plus "wrong direction" on data deltas only. 207. 2026-09-01 · **The switch was lying to screen readers** — found while rebuilding, fixed rather than logged · `role="switch"` and `aria-checked` sat on a decorative `<span>` nested inside the real `<button>`, so assistive tech announced a button containing an unfocusable switch and the on/off state belonged to neither. The roles move onto the button itself and the track is `aria-hidden`. The mutually exclusive sets (hours, theme) gained `aria-pressed` and a labelled `role="group"`; `aria-pressed` rather than `role="radio"` on purpose, since radio semantics promise arrow-key navigation this does not implement, and a control that lies about its keyboard contract is the same bug in a new place

The steps are set for the greyscale test (strip the colour, blur to 5px, and the title still has to dominate), which is a test anyone can run and nobody needs taste to score. Scope: the roles govern COPY. The locked display scale (#201) is untouched, because hero digits, `.label-data` eyebrows and the score card are DATA, and a number is not a paragraph. 216. 2026-09-02 · **The video toggle answers from a probe, not a download** — Timothy's call ("checking whether this browser can do on-device pose detection is on for a fairly long time") · the idle screen asked "can this browser do it" by loading the whole runtime: the tasks-vision chunk, then the WASM, then the 5.5MB model, then a GPU warm-up, one after another, and only then enabled Voice + Video. Every cold open paid that, including the daily drill that opens on Voice (#68, #211) for someone who was never going to touch the camera. Two questions were fused that have costs a million times apart. `poseCapable` answers the first from feature detection (a window, a camera API, WASM) in the same tick the toggle is drawn, so the "Checking…" caption is now a single hydration frame. `loadPose` answers the second, and only once Voice + Video is the mode, warming while the screen is being read so the Rec tap joins a load already in flight instead of starting one. Inside the load the model is fetched in parallel with the runtime and handed over as bytes, where it used to be opened last from inside `createFromOptions`; and a GPU delegate that refuses (policy-locked desktops, some in-app browsers) falls back to CPU, since a slower frame rate is a mode that works and null was a mode that did not exist. A load that still fails flips the toggle to unavailable and drops that screen back to Voice without writing the preference. The rule is asserted in `lib/pose-client.test.ts`

217. 2026-09-02 · **The log has one hero and one row grammar, and its empty state is the populated state with the numbers missing** — Timothy's call ("pre data and post data is difficult to understand, it takes too long for someone to understand"), one research pass against Elevate (CLAUDE.md's reference for metric presentation) · the populated log stacked ten blocks in five visual grammars (boxed sparklines, prose, a bar chart, a dark comparison card, hairline rows) with no landing point, so the eye read all of it; the one block that answered "am I getting better" sat eighth; 512 → 643 was printed three times in three shapes; every before-and-after showed two endpoints and an arrow and left the subtraction to the reader; the recording rows repeated four unit words per line with nothing aligned; and #213's empty state drew a score card the populated log never rendered, so the shape it promised was not the shape it delivered. Elevate's performance screen is one score with its trend, then every skill in one repeated row, detail only on tap; brand.md already said it ("a training log, not a report card") and so did COPY-RULES ("a number beats a line"). Now three bands, same in both states. The hero is the score card Home draws, lifted into `components/ScoreCard.tsx` so the shape is learned once, with "Day 1 scored 512." as its footer so the delta has its anchor. "What moved" is one table, header once, one row per metric: label, day one in muted, now in 17px/800, the SIGNED change in sage or rust (#195), the trend in a fixed track (`Sparkline` gains a bare mode). Words / min prints its zone ("in 130–160", "over 160") in place of a distance to a target the reader could not see. Presence and the skill lines (#69, #200) are the same row with a PRO chip for free accounts, so the teaser and the real thing share a shape. One insight line survives, the highest-ranked; the filler heatmap moves one tap down from the Fillers row it explains. "Every recording" becomes columns (date, lesson, index, fill, wpm, held) with the units in a header and the lesson name in the row, which mechanics.md always listed and the row never carried; duration lives on the full result. The comparison card leaves the log: the table is the same claim in the screen's own grammar, and the card stays on the results walk, where it is the share asset (#200's flag on its free rendering in the log closes with it). The rules, locked: a data screen has one hero and it is never a chart; a summary screen's metrics share one row grammar; a delta is printed, never implied; units are stated once, in a header; a "better" colour must be inferable from the row; pre-data draws the post-data layout with dashes; anything needing its own shape is detail, one tap down. The row arithmetic lives in `lib/log.ts`, asserted in `lib/log.test.ts`

218. 2026-09-02 · **Nothing outlined is transparent** — Timothy's call ("the cards are fine, but boxes with outlines and transparent insides are not fine") · #201's handoff said surfaces are separated by hairlines rather than fill changes, and the port took it literally: the card on /you, the shop items, the empty and error primitives, the boxed sparkline, the heatmap, the share frame, the glyph tiles on Tools and /you, and half the secondary buttons were a 1px `edge` line drawn on the ground with the room showing through, while the other half of the buttons, every input and the mod, lexicon and topic cards already carried a fill. Two looks for one role is the tell. The rule, one line: an outline always has a material behind it. Containers are `raised` with the 1px `edge` outline (the fill the filled cards already used), secondary controls and unselected segmented options are `surface` with the `stone-200` outline (the fill the filled buttons already used), the score card stays deep sage. A hairline with nothing inside it is a rule between rows, never the edge of a box, so the hairline lists (#201) are untouched. Tokens are untouched too: the step `surface` and `raised` already are is the lift, in both themes. `scripts/audit-tells.sh` gains the tell under B (a rounded, outlined element with no `bg-` on it), so it cannot come back quietly

219. 2026-09-02 · **The meter answers the voice, and the scoring wait shows the recording being read** — Timothy's call ("when you talk the bars move, when it loads there's a loading animation") · the meter existed and looked dead for two reasons. Its gain was a fixed ×4 on the RMS, so ordinary speech on a phone mic pushed the bars to a fifth of their height and a whisper and a shout looked alike; and every audio frame went through React state, re-rendering the whole rep screen sixty times a second, which on the phones this is for meant the bars lagged the voice they were meant to follow. Now `components/LevelMeter.tsx` owns the bars: the recorder writes one normalised number per frame into a ref, the meter reads it on its own animation frame and moves the bars by `transform` only (DESIGN-RULES, motion), and the screen never re-renders for audio. The normalisation is adaptive to THIS recording, the decaying loudest moment as full scale and the quietest as floor, so a quiet room and a loud tram both fill the meter; it is display only, and the engine's envelope keeps its fixed gain because `lib/envelope.ts` calibrates that against itself (#122). Bars grow from the centre, square-cornered (#201), and never vanish: silence is a flat line. The scoring wait was three lines of text. It now draws the envelope the meter just captured, bucketed to 48 bars and scaled to its own peak, with a scan line sweeping across it on the skeleton's 1.5s tempo: the person watches the shape they just made being gone through, which is what is happening, rather than a spinner standing in for nothing. A retry from the outbox has no envelope, so the track is flat and the line still sweeps. Reduced motion keeps the meter (it is data: is the mic hearing me?) at a twelfth of the rate, and stills the scan line

220. 2026-09-02 · **The UX audit's fix batch, from the first end-to-end walkthrough (docs/ux-audit-2026-09-02.md)** · one behaviour change and a set of corrections, each traced to a rule that already existed. The behaviour change: **the debrief's primary exit reads the path's `nextLesson`, the same function the floor and the road read** (amends the mechanism of #105, not its intent), because the two primary taps disagreed after every 1★ recording ("Next lesson · Kill 'like'" against "Extra lesson · The baseline · Go again"); when the path's answer is the lesson just done the button says "Go again" and the duplicate "Retake this one" hides. Whether the path should pin a lesson until 3★ at all is not decided here (needs-judgment #5). The corrections: `fetchReps`/`fetchRep` throw on an answered Supabase error so #147's error card appears instead of the zero state (a 500 rendered "Day one starts today."); the paywall takes `onUnlocked` and the debrief and the day-3 moment pass their own continuation, since `location.reload()` on /rep threw the debrief away; `maximumScale: 1` leaves the viewport (pinch-zoom is a WCAG floor, and the content is numbers); the pause bar's ticks shrink so the row fits every phone (it clipped the last ~9s of a 52s recording at 390px); the scoring wait says "Still trying" from the second attempt (the ladder ran 43s under "ten seconds or so"); the "AI-generated feedback" caption renders only with a coach; the stored-recording page counts its words; "rep" leaves the auth copy with its number agreement; "1 recordings"/"A 8-day" agree; the delta is signed once (the arrow); gated games wear the picker's "premium" chip; focus lands on the step button when the celebration ends; primary buttons carry a transparent border and the data bars opt out of forced-color adjustment; back links, home's text buttons and the road rows reach 44px. The contrast floor (H-5) and the rep-flow shapes are logged, not changed: both are palette or designer decisions under #165/#201/#203

221. 2026-09-12 · **Motion is continuity, and it is a system** — Timothy's call ("the app feels vibe coded and not premium. even messy apps like instagram feel premium. I think we need more animations"), translated per #180 before any code · what Instagram has that Ethos didn't is not more movement, it is that nothing on it teleports: sheets slide, likes bloom, screens push, every state change is continuous with the one before it. Ethos moved in exactly six places (a press scale, a chevron, two loading sweeps, the count-up, the celebration's fade) and every other change on every screen was a cut. So the rule, one line: **a state change on screen shows where the new state came from, what caused it, or what was earned, and never anything else**, inside the rules that already stood (transform and opacity only, 200ms default, 300 ceiling, 600 for the celebration, one entrance per screen, no bounce). Tokens: the durations and easings are mirrored from `lib/motion.ts` into `app/globals.css` as `--duration-*` and `--ease-*`, `lib/motion.test.ts` asserts the two copies are equal, and the same test fails on any `duration-500` typed into a component; `--ease-out` replaces Tailwind's so every `ease-out` utility is ours, and the transition defaults make a bare `transition-colors` the 120ms press step rather than Tailwind's 150. Named classes, one per job (`.arrive`, `.arrive-x`, `.reveal`, `.fill`, `.star-land`, `.sheet-panel`/`.sheet-scrim`, `.rec-ring`, `.dur-*`), so a component names the decision it is making rather than a number. **Reduced motion becomes one attribute**: `data-motion="reduce"` on `<html>`, set before paint by the boot script from the OS preference or the switch in Settings, which until now only the JavaScript animations could hear; under it every entrance is a plain fade, every value is already there, nothing slides (DESIGN-RULES DoD #8). Tab switches stay instant on purpose: the rule against animating high-frequency navigation stands, and Instagram's own tab bar cuts

222. 2026-09-12 · **Sheets rise from the bottom edge and leave the way they came** · reference: Airbnb's sheets (CLAUDE.md's reference for forms): the panel rises on its own curve, the scrim fades on its own, and closing plays in reverse rather than vanishing. `<Overlay>` keeps the sheet mounted with `data-closing` until the exit animation ends (a timer floors it for a browser that never fires the event) and only then calls `onClose`, so a parent's conditional render unmounts a sheet that has already gone; Escape, the scrim and any button inside all go through the same door. In at the ceiling (300ms, ease-out: quick off the edge, gentle landing), out at the default (200ms, the symmetric curve). The full-screen variant (the celebration) fades in and closes at once, because it owns its own slow fade (#34). Fixed on the way: the overlay's setup effect keyed on the parent's `onClose` arrow, which every parent passes fresh each render, so it re-ran and re-focused the panel on every keystroke in the code field; the handler is read through a ref now

232. 2026-09-12 · **The introduction asks five questions and answers with a portfolio** — Timothy's approval of the Phase 1 onboarding plan, built inside #11 and #133 rather than over them · the resentment #11 recorded was Wellspoken's long quiz in front of a paywall; the persona self-diagnoses (vision.md), so asking what they noticed is the app listening, not selling. The shape keeps every rule that made #11 right: five screens after the three intro screens, one question each, every answer a tap (no sliders, no text: a keyboard before the first recording costs the funnel and a slider's precision is fake), a way back on every screen, Skip on every question (the four essential ones hold "Next" until an answer; the optional one doesn't), no account, no paywall behind any of it, and the mic never more than a tap away. The questions: age band (the one thing it changes is the prompt pool, and the screen says so), what you want this for (picks the boss and the headline), what you notice when you talk (up to three; the first names the first number and the unit for it), how much you've practised (sets the frame step and whether unit intros show), and where it matters most (optional; the pool). Not asked: when you'll practise (#136 asks it after the first recording, where the OS permission prompt belongs) and anything about identity (#15). **The portfolio** is the first month's plan in their words: the goal's headline, then three lines in the screen template (day one is the baseline; the first number, named from the pain's measured dimension; the unit for what they noticed with the road's own gate), and the boss as fine print. Every number is the road's; it promises nothing about the outcome. **The mapping is one editable table**, `content/portfolio.ts`, and `lib/portfolio.test.ts` holds it to the road (every unit and boss must exist) and to the copy rules; `lib/portfolio.ts` builds from it, pure, with a rules version so stored plans can be rebuilt. What an answer may do: name the first number, name the unit and its gate, pick the boss, set two defaults (frame step, unit intros), narrow the roulette's pool (school drops the three job prompts), and put the focus metric first on the day-3 card. What it never does: score, reorder the road, pick the lesson, or award a star (#10, #46). **Storage**: the answers and the step live on the device first (`ethos.onboarding`, because they exist before a session does, and a refresh resumes where it was), and a finished walk is upserted to `public.onboarding` (migration 0009: one row per user, closed sets, RLS to the owner, the built portfolio and its rules version alongside) once a session exists; a device with no answers takes the account's down (`lib/answers-sync.ts`, the freeze-sync pattern). The plan is reachable again from /you's plan row, which reopens the walk at the plan or at any question, because an answer is allowed to change

Radius one step warmer at 12/16/20, with no concentric arithmetic, because inner = outer − inset computes to 0 at every padding in this app, which is how the 4px corner on the coach bubble happened. The rule that actually matters is that only one of them is white. **Press drops one level and springs back**: 60ms down on the plain curve because contact is not an animation, then the base duration up on `--ease-spring` (cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.56, 0.64, 1), about 6% of overshoot), which is the whole "it responds to your hand" feeling in four CSS rules. A level-0 control has nothing to drop, so the scale and the veil are its whole press: a text link that cast a shadow on release would be inventing depth it never had. A level-2 surface that ARRIVES rather than being there on load takes `.arrive-lift`, which comes in from 6px below and 1.5% small, so the thing that matters rises into place instead of sliding into it.

242. 2026-09-12 · **The motion layer: a screen arrives from the direction you travelled, a list assembles, a sheet follows the finger** · Job 3's machinery, so the per-screen work is application rather than invention. **The push**: the app has a left and a right — along the tab bar follows the tabs, deeper comes from the right, back comes from the left — because by the time the direction is wrong so is the user's model of where they are. React 19 stable does not export the view-transition component Next's `viewTransition` flag needs (it is on the experimental channel), so what ships is the incoming half: 24px of travel with the opacity ramp finished in the first third, which reads as motion with a direction rather than as a dissolve; `components/PageTransition.tsx` is the one place that changes when the API lands. **The stagger**: `.stagger` on a container lands its rows 40ms apart via `nth-child`, capped at the eighth, because a true ladder down 29 road rows takes 1.2 seconds and stops being an entrance; `nth-child` rather than a `--i` on every row, because a stagger that needs an inline style per child is a stagger nobody applies to the second list. A container already inside an `.arrive` gives the block fade up: one entrance per block, never two. **The drag**: while a pointer is down the sheet tracks it with no easing and no duration at all, because a panel that lags your thumb by 200ms is a panel you are not dragging; a fifth of its height or a flick faster than half a pixel a millisecond sends it away, anything less springs home. The check walks from whatever was touched up to the panel looking for anything already scrolled, since the paywall puts its own overflow on the card inside the panel and dragging a sheet you were reading is how you lose your place. **The Record button breathes** at 2% on a 2.6s cycle while it waits: the one ambient loop outside the introduction and a live recording, and it earns it the way they do, by being the screen's only job. Two harnesses: `scripts/check-motion-layer.mjs` asserts all of it in a browser, `scripts/strip.mjs` photographs a transition at 0/80/160/240/400ms. One cost worth knowing: an ambient loop on a primary control makes Playwright's click wait forever for the element to hold still.

243. 2026-09-12 · **Demos hears you** · Job 4's first half, and the most important animation in the app. He was a still WebP beside a live meter, which on a speaking app says the numbers are alive and the character is a sticker. He reacts now, off the same ref the meter reads: ears lift and spread with volume, the eyes sweep with the waveform (faster and wider the louder it gets, so quiet speech is a flicker and a raised voice is a full look across), a lean in when speech starts, a settle about half a second after it stops, and a blink on his own clock between two and six seconds, because a thing that never blinks is dead. Drawn in VECTOR rather than animated from the art: the shipped Demos is a WebP with one flat alpha and no amount of CSS lifts an ear in a raster, so this is the same panda re-drawn at the palette sampled straight out of `assets/demos-onboard-listening.png` (#d05c37 fur, #58271a ears and limbs, the cream markings), cropped to head and shoulders because that is the part of him that listens. Never re-renders: everything is a transform written to the DOM on an animation frame, the pattern `LevelMeter` already uses, because sixty state updates a second through the rep screen is what made the meter stutter on the phones this is for. Reduced motion keeps the one thing that is information — he is hearing you — and drops the sweep, the breath, the lean and the blink.

252. 2026-09-13 · **The ring, and what it is honestly allowed to claim** · `components/Ring.tsx`. One closed loop wherever a number has a ceiling. `pathLength={1}` is the whole trick: the browser rescales the dash units to the path, so the arc length IS the fraction and no circumference arithmetic appears anywhere, which also means a value change is a plain CSS transition between two dash lengths and therefore travels from where it was. It buzzes on the EDGE of closing, once, never on arrival at a ring that was already full, because a phone that buzzes on arrival is a phone that buzzes for nothing. Where the population data behind the scale is provisional the TROUGH goes dashed and the arc stays solid: what somebody did is known even when the place it sits is a guess, and a dashed ring says "we are estimating" in the picture rather than in a footnote nobody reads. **What it is not claimed to do**: `docs/closure.md` went looking for evidence that a closing circle motivates differently from a bar at the same percentage and found none at all, so the ring is argued on bounded legibility — it packs a finite remainder into a square tile beside its own number, which a bar cannot do at 64px — and nothing else. One cost worth recording: the class was called `ring`, which is a Tailwind utility that paints a square box-shadow, so every ring in the app wore a cream rectangle and nothing in the computed border, outline or box-shadow explained it.

253. 2026-09-13 · **Four streak tiers, and the top one has no ceiling** · Spark 1 to 9, Flame 10 to 30, Torch 31 to 75, Beacon 76 and on, with a mark each (`IconSpark`, the existing `IconFlame`, `IconTorch`, `IconBeacon`) that escalates as one object rather than four unrelated glyphs. A counter that only counts has nothing to cross, and "day 47" is not a bounded remainder; four tiers give a long streak something to be part way through. The boundaries are where they are for reasons: 10 is past the point most people quit, 31 is the first streak worth losing, and the fourth tier is open-ended because a ceiling on a habit is a reason to stop and somebody 400 days in does not need a new badge. Day 0 is not a tier: a person with no streak is not a Spark waiting to happen, and dressing that up is the manufactured feeling vision.md rules out.

254. 2026-09-13 · **The clean run: the one speech number a body already owns** · `lib/clean-run.ts`, the longest filler-free stretch of a recording, computed from the filler timestamps every recording already stores, so it is free and retroactive. Every other measure in Ethos is a rate or a position; this one is a DURATION, and nobody needs to be told whether forty seconds is more than thirty. Both ends count, because the clean run-up to somebody's first stumble is the same achievement as the same stretch in the middle. It deliberately excludes self-corrections: those are counted but not timestamped, and a number that silently ignores half of what it claims to measure is worse than a narrower number that is exactly true.

255. 2026-09-13 · **Three first cards, rendered side by side rather than argued about** · `/workbench/home-cards`, a route that is not in the nav and is not an app screen. The brief asked for a metric a BODY understands instead of an index, and three options rendered before choosing: **the standing** (a percentile, instantly understandable but still a composite, which is the Ethos Index wearing a friendlier unit and hiding which number moved), **the clean run** (a duration, one trait, a personal best rather than a rank, but it moves in lumps because one stray um at second 31 halves it) and **the tell** (a rate turned into the gap between, a rhythm you can hear yourself doing, but it leads with the thing you are worst at every single day, which is the one that could read as a scold). All three share one shell on purpose: the comparison is between the metrics, and three different card designs would be comparing three different card designs. The workbench stays, because the alternative — build one, screenshot it, replace it, build the next — compares each option against a memory of the last one.

256. 2026-09-13 · **The percentile maths, and the file that refuses to let an invented one ship** · `lib/percentile.ts` holds the arithmetic and three decisions that make it honest. **Direction**, because fewer fillers is better and a raw percentile would tell somebody the opposite. **Bands**, because 210 words a minute beats 97% of people and that is both true and useless, so a banded trait is ranked on distance from the middle of its band and the percentile answers "how many people are further out than you". **Shape**, because a normal fitted to a rate that cannot go below zero puts a quarter of the population there and everybody left scores above the 50th. `content/norms.ts` holds the distributions with their citations and assumptions, and `lib/norms.test.ts` fails on any norm carrying placeholder numbers, by name. A percentile is a claim about everybody else, and an invented one looks exactly like a working feature on a phone; the gate is the only thing standing between those two states.

257. 2026-09-13 · **Traits replace the road, and the next lesson is whichever number is lowest** · `content/traits.ts` (what each trait is, why it matters, the distinction the lesson turns on, the tactics, and the one concrete move in that trait's own units) and `lib/trait-readings.ts` (the raw extractor per trait, the percentile, and `nextTrait`). There is no fixed order any more and no gate: the only reason a lesson is the one in front of you is that its number was your lowest, and the screen says so. Pausing is measured as LANDED pauses a minute rather than all pauses, because placement is the skill and a count including the searching ones would go up when somebody got worse. **A provisional trait cannot be the reason for a lesson** — sending somebody somewhere on the strength of a percentile the evidence does not support is the one dishonest thing the whole model exists to avoid. Two things worth knowing: `lib/traits.ts` already existed and meant something else (nine Index dimensions as profile levels, which the shift retires), so this lives beside it under a name that says which one it is; and `repairs_per_min` is not a column, so it is recovered by inverting `repairScore`, exact to about 0.015 a minute and lower-bounded at the floor. The right fix is a column and it should happen before this ships to anyone.

258. 2026-09-13 · **One lesson, end to end, and the recorder can now hand control back** · `/practice/[trait]`, six steps: name the trait with its percentile, why a listener cares, the tactics, the walk-through, sixty seconds, and then the ring MOVES in front of them. The walk-through is the worked example Timothy named and it is the whole reason the lesson is a lesson rather than a tip: the same sentence with the same 1.4 seconds of silence in two places, so "a pause to think and a pause for effect sound identical, only the placement differs" stops being an assertion and becomes something you can hear. `repHref` takes a `back` now and the recorder honours it on both exits, so a lesson that sends somebody out to record gets them back on its own last screen rather than on the floor; same-origin paths only, since a redirect target taken from a query string is the oldest hole there is. The example step is skipped for a trait without approved copy rather than filled with something written on the spot (#248's pattern). The last screen compares against the recording BEFORE this one rather than a stored snapshot, because a snapshot goes stale the moment somebody records outside the lesson and then the screen claims a change that did not happen here.

259. 2026-09-13 · **The closure research, and the famous mechanism failing** · `docs/closure.md`, three researchers on task closure, goal gradients and rings in practice, reading primary papers with instructions to say loudly where a repeated claim turned out to be poorly supported. **Zeigarnik does not survive**: pooled recall ratio 0.99 across 38 publications, 0.99 again with Zeigarnik's own outlying 1927 study removed, and it goes NEGATIVE (0.88) in achievement-scored settings, which is what Ethos is by construction. So no cliffhangers, no truncated results, no half-finished card. What holds is less decorative: progress monitoring raises goal attainment across 138 randomised trials, N = 19,951, d+ = 0.40, which is Ethos's premise already right — with two moderators that change the build, that the number must be RECORDED and that monitoring an outcome moves outcomes while monitoring a behaviour moves behaviour, so one number cannot do both jobs. **A percentile is not a closable loop**: Apple's rings close because Move, Exercise and Stand are behaviours you control in the next hour, and every non-Apple competitor ships a state display that never closes plus an explicit rest mode. **The boomerang is the biggest risk and it hits the best users**: a bare descriptive norm made above-average households use more energy, and an injunctive cue removed it, so a trait card must never be only a position. Sixteen mechanisms to use with their effect sizes, eleven to reject with the reason.

260. 2026-09-13 · **The ring is drawn from um and uh alone, and per hundred words** · `lib/metrics.ts` gains `filledPauseCount` and `filledPer100`, and `lib/trait-readings.ts` reads the Fillers trait off the second of those. The distribution research came back with a blocker that is not about sample size: a **construct mismatch**. Every published rate it could reach (Bortfeld 2001, Shriberg 1996, Clark and Fox Tree 2002) counts filled pauses and nothing else, while `fillersPerMin` also counts like, you know, sort of, kind of, basically, actually and literally. Placing somebody against one set while measuring the other is a subtraction between two different quantities wearing the word percentile, and it fails worst on exactly the people Ethos serves: Tagliamonte found 8 of 18 Toronto speakers aged 10 to 19 used "like" more often than "and", so a 17 year old would be told they are unusually disfluent for saying a word their whole year level says. **Per hundred WORDS rather than per minute** for a second reason from the same research: per minute rewards talking faster, and the same speaker at 120 and at 160 words a minute, saying "um" exactly as often per sentence, appears to have cut a third of them. There is a test for that. What does NOT change: `fillersPerMin`, `fillerScore` and the Index are untouched, and the discourse markers stay counted and timestamped on the results screen. They are real and users care about them; they are just not something a position can be claimed about. No migration, because every stored hit already carries the word it matched.

261. 2026-09-13 · **The shift's camera, and light beside dark rather than after it** · `scripts/look-shift.mjs` shoots the four things the shift introduces at the same 390px and the same two themes as `look.mjs`, and `scripts/gallery-shift.mjs` composes them. Two details worth keeping: the camera waits a beat past the skeleton check, because a ring starts at zero and travels on the next frame and a shot taken on arrival photographs five empty troughs; and the sheet puts light and dark side by side in each row instead of running one theme after the other, because the comparison worth making is between the themes and not between the halves of a long scroll. A row of one shot per theme leaves most of the sheet empty, so its caption stands in that space, and the before shot is cropped to the part its row is arguing about rather than running its full scroll and dwarfing everything it is being compared against.

262. 2026-09-13 · **All five scales are provisional, and that is the finding** · `content/norms.ts`, `docs/percentiles.md`. Four researchers on the primary literature, one per measurable trait, then an adversarial auditor per proposal whose only job was to verify every citation exists and says what it was claimed to say, redo the arithmetic, and decide whether the model was being sold as firmer than the evidence supports. Every proposal came back the same shape: **the centres are published and convergent, the between-speaker SPREAD is not**, and a percentile is mostly made of the spread. Pausing has the best-evidenced thresholds in the engine (De Jong and Bosker tested every cut-off from 20 ms to 1000 ms and found 250 to 300 optimal; placement-over-presence is the oldest replicated result in the field) and no speaker-level distribution anywhere, because the field reports distributions OF PAUSES rather than of speakers. Pace is four-ways convergent on 148 words a minute and its spread is two indirect estimates agreeing. Variety cannot use a published figure at all: type-token ratio falls as a sample grows, so a corpus number is a different measurement wearing the same name, which is the same class of error as the filler construct mismatch caught one trait earlier. **The one audit that ran before the session limit changed both of its trait's parameters and caught the proposal misreporting its own evidence**: it had claimed no published dispersion exists for English filled pauses, where Clark and Fox Tree section 7.3 gives 65 English speakers, 1.2 to 88.5 per 1,000 words, median 17.3. That error was not safe in either direction, since it licensed borrowing a spread from a Hungarian secondary source and the borrowed value was the FLOOR of what the English data supports. Centre corrected 4.53 → 3.0 per hundred words and treated as a ceiling; spread corrected 0.75 → 1.0. Three audits never ran and `docs/percentiles.md` says so in its method section rather than omitting it. **Three findings here are about constants the app already ships and none of them waits on a ring**: `fillerScore` pins the median speaker at 15 out of 100 and everyone above the 75th at zero, `repairScore` scores the average human 3 to 16, and the 130 to 160 pace zone is the 26th to the 65th percentile and charges an energetic 180 words a minute exactly what it charges a laboured 110, which the perception literature does not support on the fast side at all. The band stays where it is for now because moving half of it would leave the app with two zones disagreeing about what "in the zone" means.

263. 2026-09-13 · **A provisional scale does not get to say "the 40th"** · the trait card already said "scale provisional" and drew a dashed trough, and the lesson built on that same trait printed "40 PERCENTILE" at 132px, then "Your lowest trait. 40th percentile" underneath, then "Pausing, up 26." on the last screen. A dashed ring is a good signal and it is not licence for the copy beside it to assert the thing the dash is hedging. Where the scale is provisional both screens now lead with the number that IS known: the measurement is not the doubtful half, somebody held 1.8 pauses a minute whatever the population does, and the last screen compares the raw values rather than the positions because "up 26" is a claim about everybody else. One definition of "the measurement, printed, with its unit" now lives in `trait-readings` as `withUnit`, and the singular follows the DISPLAYED value, since 0.997 prints as "1" and then read "1 restarts a minute". Also: the look fixture's transcript was 42 tokens against a row claiming 128 words a minute over 58 seconds, and because distinct words per hundred rises as a sample shrinks, that one bad fixture photographed the Variety ring pinned at 100 on every screen in the gallery. It is 143 tokens now, which is 148 words a minute over the same 58 seconds and happens to land on the median the pace literature converges on.

264. 2026-09-13 · **Four audits, four proposals caught overclaiming, and three shipped constants indicted** · the adversarial half of the percentile research is the half that earned its cost. Every audit found its proposal more confident than its own evidence, and every overclaim was invisible on a reread. **Fillers** said no published dispersion exists for English filled pauses, in a paper it claimed to have read in full whose section 7.3 gives 65 English speakers and a median, and that error is what licensed borrowing a spread from a Hungarian secondary source at the FLOOR of what the English data supports. **Pace** built its only direct words-a-minute measurement on a percentile label its source attaches to different numbers, and called an 11% correction unstated when the cited paper states it outright; sweeping the spread across the range it calls plausible moves somebody at 127 words a minute between the 10th percentile and the 32nd, so the verdict became "ship the band change, do not ship the percentile". **Pausing** was the worst: a citation fabricated at the author level, a central reason for refusal that was false and checkable, a headline convergence that was an artifact of three incompatible thresholds, and its one concrete code change reversing sign under the better-matched corpus. **Pitch** was the only conclusion to survive whole and still lost its distribution, for treating reproducibility as validity. What comes out of it: three constants the app already ships are wrong in ways that do not wait on any ring (`fillerScore` pins the median speaker at 15 out of 100, `repairScore` scores the average human 3 to 16, the pace zone is the 26th to the 65th percentile and charges an energetic 180 words a minute what it charges a laboured 110), and `detectRepairs` has never done what its own name says. The rule held: where an audit disagreed with a proposal, the audit won, and `docs/percentiles.md` records the disagreement rather than folding it in.

265. 2026-09-13 · **The word goes, the threshold stays** · `RHETORICAL_MIN_S` is `LANDING_MIN_S`. 0.8 seconds buys a boundary pause rather than a rhetorical one, and no better line is defensible today: one corpus puts 24% of all pauses at or above it where the genuine long class is 14%, and the corpus that actually matches Ethos's speakers puts it at 9%. Moving the line on that evidence would be picking a side of a twofold disagreement, so the line stays and the claim changes. A landing at a boundary is worth rewarding under either reading and it is what the function measures. Also in this pass: the dashed trough, which is the ring saying "this scale is an estimate" in the picture rather than in a footnote, had almost no contrast on the dark card, because a dashed stroke carries about half the ink of a solid one at the same colour. It has its own token now. The first attempt made it worse by declaring that token inside `@theme`, where Tailwind v4 tree-shakes any theme variable no utility references: the light value never reached the build, `var()` resolved to nothing and the trough computed to `stroke: none`, so light lost its dashes while dark gained them. Caught by putting the two themes side by side and then reading the computed stroke instead of guessing at it.

266. 2026-09-13 · **A percentile never stands on its own, including at the top** · `nextLine` in `lib/trait-readings.ts`, and it returns null for exactly one reason. The synthesis pass over the three closure researchers (`docs/closure.md`, now 21 mechanisms with a CONFIDENCE column and 15 rejections) names this as a hard invariant and asks for a test, and the test found the hole: `move` gives up at the top of a trait, so a non-provisional reading at the 99th percentile rendered a naked position. That is the exact failure the boomerang describes, and it damages the people already doing well: a bare descriptive norm made above-average households use MORE energy, and an injunctive cue removed the effect. At the top of a trait the honest instruction is not a bigger number, it is holding the one you have, which is an injunctive cue in the sense the research means. The card and the lesson read one function now instead of assembling the line twice, and `lib/trait-readings.test.ts` asserts it across every trait at every percentile from 0 to 100. Two other rules from the same synthesis, checked rather than assumed: **do not cite a company's own engagement statistics as evidence** (Apple's ring-closure health figures appear in `closure.md` twice and both are rejections of them), and **aggregate percentiles on the population rather than on averaged per-user ratios**, which `lib/percentile.ts` already does by construction since a norm is population parameters. The synthesis also killed one mechanic that had looked attractive: switching a ring's label from "3 done" to "2 to go" past halfway, whose parent result came back p = .758 with the sign reversed at N = 768,703.

267. 2026-09-14 · **Today is composed of what a person is about to do and the five numbers that said so** · the Ethos Index left the first card for `/history`. A single number out of 1000 is the most compressible thing on the screen and the least actionable: it moves for five reasons at once, so it can never tell anyone what to do next, and it sat where the day's first action belongs. What replaced it is a pair. **The clean run** is the headline metric — the longest stretch of a recording with no filler, no repair and no searching pause, drawn as a ring against that recording's OWN duration rather than against a constant, which is the bug the card shipped with: `DAILY_MAX_SECONDS` is 90 and the ring was scaled to 60, so every clean run past a minute drew past full. It is the right headline because it is a fact about one recording, it is countable by ear, and it gets better in a way you can feel. Under it, **the five trait rings**, each carrying its own raw measurement and the dashed provisional trough where the scale is still an estimate. Between them they answer "how did it go" and "what is my worst thing" without either answer needing a composite. The Index still exists and is still stored; it is now a thing you go and look at rather than a thing that greets you.

268. 2026-09-14 · **The first card was unreadable for a structural reason, not a copy reason** · it took its title from `nextLesson(starsByLesson(history))` and its reason from `nextFocus(history)`. Two selectors, run independently, over two different vocabularies — the road's units and the trait names — with `content/traits.ts` quietly a third. So the card would name a lesson about pausing and then explain, underneath, that the recommendation was about fillers, and both halves were telling the truth. No amount of rewriting the strings fixes a card whose two lines are chosen by different functions. `lib/next-practice.ts` is now the one selector: `choosePractice` ranks the five traits on their RAW measurement (not their percentile, because every scale is provisional and a provisional percentile cannot be compared across traits), picks the weakest, and returns one `Chosen` that the title, the reason, the prompt and the trait strip all read. The card's hero is now the PROMPT — the thing you are about to say — and not a number, because the five numbers are already on the screen below it and a bigger copy of one of them is a duplicate, not a headline. The terms sit above the button rather than under it ("A prompt, up to 90 seconds of speaking, then your five numbers"), so the deal is legible before the tap rather than after it. Day one keeps its own shape and its own words, which the first version of this card deleted and two of the 41 onboarding checks caught.

269. 2026-09-14 · **Lessons and the path are two different things and were one thing for too long** · **Lessons** are fifteen, three per trait, each with three practices, each with its own commissioned picture, and you choose them. **The path** is 120 pre-determined practices — twelve angles per trait across two hidden tiers — that the daily card draws from so that Today reads as generated for you. Neither is the road. The tier is never rendered and never named; it decides which of two shapes a practice takes and nothing else. The last practice of a lesson is harder than the two before it, and "harder" is a MOD rather than a longer timer or a higher bar: `no-notes` hides the prompt the moment recording starts, which is the cheapest real difficulty in the app and is already balanced. It is always the FREE mod. A lesson whose last step could not be reached without paying would be progress sold for money, and #14 rules that out; the three premium mods stay optional and on top. Progress is DERIVED from `lesson_id` on stored recordings (`lesson:<id>:<n>`) rather than tracked beside them — there is no lesson-progress table and there should not be one, because a counter that can drift from the recordings will, and the recordings are what the product is made of. The art is fifteen risograph pieces, generated on warm grounds with an explicit colour-exclusion list, cut to 900×585 webp at about 130KB each. Timothy approved the set, so the style is closed.

270. 2026-09-14 · **The PWA has had no offline shell since #249 and the reason is one word in the spec** · `cache.addAll()` is atomic. One 404 in the list rejects the whole promise and NOTHING is cached — and the install handler swallowed it with `.catch(() => {})`, so the failure was silent and the app looked installed. The dead entry was `demos-workout.webp`, a file the banned-word pass renamed and the shell list did not follow. Now every entry is cached on its own (`Promise.all(SHELL.map((u) => c.add(u).catch(() => {})))`), so a missing asset costs that asset instead of the whole app, and the shell carries `/lessons` and the fifteen pictures. The general rule: an atomic primitive plus a swallowed rejection is a feature that reports success and does nothing, and it will not be found by looking at the feature.

271. 2026-09-14 · **Two bugs that were live, one of them an hour old** · `resolveRepConfig` fell back to `todaysDrill(now)` for any lesson id it did not recognise. Since #258 the trait walk records with `?lesson=trait-pause`, which is not a drill id, so every one of those recordings showed the wrong prompt and filed its stars against whichever road lesson the rotation happened to name — opening unit gates nobody had earned. An unknown id is now `practice:<id>`, which resolves to a real prompt and files against itself. Second, the introduction gate had stopped being a first-run gate in #268's edit and was handing every user `/lesson/filler` every day. `introOwns` is gated on `history.length === 0` again. Both were found by an Explore pass over what the Today restructure stranded, not by a test, and the lesson to keep is that a fallback which silently substitutes a DIFFERENT valid value is worse than one that fails: the app never looked broken, it just attributed work to the wrong place.

272. 2026-09-14 · **The road is demoted, not retired, and here is the inventory so the teardown starts from a list** · `/lessons` is the Lessons page now and `PathRoad` is no longer a surface anyone lands on, but `UNITS` and `DRILLS` still back the debrief's fallback, the star map, the unit gates and six test files. Full retirement is about fifteen source files and six red test files, which is its own job and not this one. What MOVED: the "You said rushing" mark (#231, and in the 41/41 gate) is on the Lessons page, in the user's own words. What was DROPPED: the road's endowed "Showed up" row, and that is a correction rather than a loss — `docs/closure.md`'s reject list opens with endowed progress, which works precisely BECAUSE the head start is unearned, and CLAUDE.md says stars, streaks and scores are earned. What is LEFT to tear down: `lib/drills.ts`, `content/units.ts` and their consumers, `nextLesson`/`starsByLesson`, `components/home/PathRoad.tsx`, the `/path` route, and the six test files that assert road behaviour.

273. 2026-09-14 · **A lesson ends as a lesson** · the debrief's loudest button was always "Next lesson · <the road's next unit>", including at the end of practice one of three. So the app's own recommendation was to abandon the lesson it had just started you on. The `game` branch had already worked this out — #194, a game ends as a game — and the rule generalises: whatever sent you here decides what "forward" means. A lesson practice now offers **Practice 2 of 3** of the SAME lesson as its primary, with the return to the lesson as the quiet exit; the last practice offers the return as the primary; the trait walk's closing screen, which is the whole reason that walk exists, gets the same treatment. The road's next unit is not offered at all from inside a lesson. `scripts/check-lessons.mjs` walks Lessons → a lesson → a recording → the debrief and asserts both halves of the join: that the recording filed as `lesson:the-cold-open:1`, and that the loudest button is the next practice.

274. 2026-09-14 · **Two up, and a plain `<img>`** · at full width each lesson card was most of a screen and the fifteen ran to 11,544 pixels. Nothing could be compared with anything, and a commissioned set that is never seen together is fifteen unrelated pictures rather than a set. Two up halved it; giving each trait's FIRST lesson the whole row fixed what two up broke, because three cards in a two-column grid orphan the third and that hole, repeated five times down the page, read as something failing to load. The blurb went with the width — the trait's own line already sits above the grid and says what all three cards under it are for. The art is served as a plain `<img>` at its own path rather than through `next/image`, and that is about the offline shell: the service worker pre-caches `/lessons/*.webp`, while `next/image` requests `/_next/image?url=…&w=…` at a width it picks per device, which is never the URL in the shell — so the PWA would have held fifteen files the page never asked for and shown fifteen holes offline. It also fixed a thing that looked like a ghost: in dark, and only in dark, the five large images photographed as empty boxes with every request a 200 and every `naturalWidth` non-zero. Two camera fixes came out of the same hunt and stay: a full-page shot now scrolls the page first, because the browser only ever loaded what was in the viewport and eleven of fifteen pictures were blank, and it calls `decode()` on every image before the shutter, because `complete` only says the bytes arrived.

275. 2026-09-14 · **Lessons takes a tab, and the second tab list that had been waiting to catch somebody** · fifteen illustrated lessons were reachable through one text link at the foot of the trait strip, while the tab bar highlighted Today the whole time you stood in them. That is a wardrobe, not a section. Five tabs at 390px is 74.8px each, well over the 44px target floor, and `.label-micro` has no truncate so every label stays one short word. The thing worth writing down is what the change nearly stepped on: **the tab list existed in two places**. `components/Nav.tsx` drew it and `components/PageTransition.tsx` kept its own hardcoded copy to decide which way a page slides in, with nothing connecting them. A tab in the first and missing from the second gets `depth() === 1` instead of 0, so it pushes in from the right like a deeper screen and reverses on the way back, and nothing in review catches that because both files are individually correct. The hrefs are in `lib/tabs.ts` now and `lib/tabs.test.ts` holds both consumers to them IN ORDER, because the order is the part `PageTransition` reads. A happy accident worth knowing: `depth()`'s regex matches `/lesson` as a prefix, so `/lessons/<id>` still counts as the deepest thing in the app while `/lessons` itself is a tab, which is exactly right.

276. 2026-09-14 · **The floor card loses a sentence and a second button label, and COPY-RULES had already ruled on both** · the terms line ("A prompt, up to 90 seconds of speaking, then your five numbers.") went in #268 to make the tap say what it does. COPY-RULES §placement says the opposite and says it twice: default to no explanation, and "if a string exists to justify a design decision to the user, cut it". What it explained is what the next screen does, in order, thirty seconds later. And **"Go again" went with it**: on a day already spoken on, the button dropped to a label that frames a second recording as a repeat of the first, which is the one reading this card should never have. The eyebrow above it already carries the difference ("One more · Pausing" against "Today's practice · Pausing"), so the button says **Take the floor** every time and the app's own phrase stops having a second-class variant. The debrief's `{again ? "Go again" : "Next lesson"}` is a different screen and a different job and keeps its own wording.

277. 2026-09-14 · **The account ask moves to the end of the introduction, and survives the promise only because nothing on it is a gate** · Timothy's call on 14 Sep. Until now the product has never asked for an account before it worked once: #15 set that rule, the save-progress wall (#134) is where it was kept, and `/about` still prints "60 seconds. No signup until you've spoken." The new screen is the last step of the walk, after the plan, and asks to keep the plan you have just been shown, which is the one moment where an account is about something visible rather than about a future you have not had yet. It holds three rules. **Nothing is gated**: "Not now" is a plain link at full tap height, not a word swallowed in a corner, and it goes straight to the floor. **It is skipped entirely** for a signed-in, non-anonymous session, so somebody returning through "I already have an account" on screen one is not asked twice. **The wall downstream is untouched**, and needs no change, because it only ever fires for anonymous sessions: signing up here silences it for free. Google is first because `signInWithGoogle("signup")` calls `linkIdentity` on an anonymous session rather than signing into a new one, so nothing recorded on the device is orphaned. It wears the save wall's grammar rather than `/signup`'s: one terracotta tap on the thing being asked for, a neutral second door, a quiet decline that continues. No four-colour G, because that mark belongs on a light surface and this screen's one accent is the accent every screen gets. The gate is 45/45.

278. 2026-09-14 · **One reel, two callers, and the animation that called itself a reel for a month** · `components/TopicRoulette.tsx` and `app/boss/page.tsx` both drew a random topic, both described what they did as "a reel, not a swap", and neither was one: six `setState` swaps 70ms apart with the text held at 40% opacity, each draw playing the app's 6px `.arrive`. Nothing travelled. The card flickered, which reads as a page struggling rather than as a wheel being turned, and the boss's version was a near-verbatim copy so a change to one of them was a change to neither. `components/Reel.tsx` is the wheel: a strip of drawn candidates in a one-cell window, one transition, and the spring's overshoot at the end so it lands with a stop instead of arriving. Measured in the browser: six cells at 92px, 0 to −404 to −368 over 340ms. `transform` and `opacity` only, inside `DURATION.max`, because `lib/motion.ts` keeps 600ms for celebrations and a draw is not one. **There is a frame AFTER the one it settles on**, because `--ease-spring` travels past its target and comes back, and without something under the landing frame that overshoot shows the empty bottom of the window. Two bugs found by looking at it rather than by reasoning: the window had no height, so the column made the card six prompts tall and the strip travelled past the fold, and a percentage translate is a percentage of the MOVING element's own box, so −100% moved the whole strip rather than one prompt. Then the offset was stored and reset in an effect, which left one painted frame where a one-cell column was still translated four cells up: the window flashed empty for about 160ms after every landing. It is derived during render now.

279. 2026-09-14 · **You could not get out of a trait lesson** · `app/practice/[trait]/page.tsx` set `const back = i > 0 ? () => go(i - 1) : undefined`, and `/practice` is in `Nav`'s `BARE` list, which draws no tab bar. `components/LessonScreen.tsx` renders its back row only when `onBack` is truthy and the component deliberately cannot render prose, so on step 0 the only control on the page was the one that goes deeper. The screen you land on from a tap on Today was the one screen with no exit, and the browser gesture was the product's back button. `app/lesson/[unit]/page.tsx` never passed `onBack` at all, so the unit teaching screen was the same trap. Both exit to Today now. The general shape is worth keeping: a list of routes that hide the tab bar is a good idea, a component that renders a back control only when asked is a good idea, and the two of them together make a dead end that neither file looks responsible for. `lib/way-out.test.ts` asserts it per screen and fails on the `: undefined` fallback specifically.

280. 2026-09-14 · **Premium wears plum, and two of the nine treatments it replaces were saying something untrue** · premium was marked nine ways. A locked mod said "premium" in lowercase stone-400 after its name; the Log said "Pro" in a grey pill; the Presence card said nothing and drew an ellipsis; its detail card said it in a terracotta wash with a terracotta button; `/history`, `/you` and `/boss` each used a plain terracotta text link; the cap moment used a stone text button; the boss's spin quota used **sage**; the boss's weekly lock said it in prose with no mark at all; and the Hostile Q&A weekly cap did not say it anywhere, so you found out by being refused. Three names were in circulation for one tier. The two that mattered are the borrowed colours: terracotta means tap and sage means earned, and **a free allowance is allotted rather than earned**, so the spin counter in the earned colour was the palette contradicting the distinction the whole product rests on (#14: money buys cosmetics, never progress). Paid now has a colour of its own: plum, hue 288 rather than a redder one, which collapses onto terracotta for a tritanope. 700 is the text, 300 the border, 50 the wash, and there is deliberately **no fill role**, because a premium card with an action is a plum card with a terracotta button on it, which is the rule in one screenshot. One component (`components/PremiumMark.tsx`), one name ("Premium"), and one glyph: **an open door, never a padlock**, because #200 forbids a padlock over an empty box and #73 found a free user seeing the thing work is the stronger prompt, so a lock would draw the opposite of what the product believes. The mark never hides a number: the ellipsis stays, the quota still counts, the row still says how many recordings are behind it. `lib/premium-mark.test.ts` fails if a plum element also wears one of the two taken accents, if the tier's name renders in either, if plum becomes a fill, if a step is used without being declared or without a dark value, or if the door grows a third stroke. **Not changed, and flagged rather than done quietly**: the paywall sheet still wears the deep-sage material #165 called "the most premium surface the system owns". By this entry's own logic the one place money changes hands is the worst place for the earned colour, but #165 is Timothy's and the change is a before-and-after to look at rather than a thing to ship silently.

281. 2026-09-14 · **The daily challenge, and the place `docs/closure.md` contradicts itself** · rows 2, 6, 7, 8, 9 and 17 specified it, `Ring.tsx` reserved the `open` tone for it in #252, and no caller had ever passed that tone. **Row 9** says the target derives from the user's trailing seven days and must be able to go DOWN. **Row 8** says it must be closeable by an ordinary person on an ordinary day, with a 70% floor. The document's own worked example, "your own last-week median", satisfies the first and breaks the second: a median is missed half the time by construction, and §6's reject list is unambiguous about the cost. Wang et al., 95,532 loyalty members, randomised: 80% missed a target set to be impressive, and failure depressed behaviour for EIGHT MONTHS, concentrated in the most committed. So the line is not the median. It is **the value you already clear on about seven of every ten of your own recordings**: the 30th percentile of your own trailing values, by nearest rank. Still self-referential, still moves both ways, and sized by row 8's floor rather than against it. Nearest rank rather than interpolated, so the line is always a number you actually produced and "you were under it on five of your last seven" is a fact. The window ends at this week's Monday, so today's recording cannot move the line today's recording is measured against. No card under three readings, because a line from one recording is a fiction and a seeded one is the endowed progress §6 rejects. It reads the trait readings and `created_at` and nothing else, and a test passes rows carrying an Index of 999 and three stars and asserts the result is byte-identical: row 2's "two channels, never merged" stops being a promise and becomes something the module cannot do. **The prize is a coin for a week with five closed days, not one a day**: the streak-day coin already fires for the recording that closes a challenge, so a daily one is two ledger rows for one act and doubles the rate `FIRST_SHOP_ITEM_PRICE` was set backwards from. Migration 0011 is small because 0005 had already made the unique index full; only 0004's insert policy had to widen, and it can verify a week holds real recordings but not that a challenge closed, which is the same division of labour the streak coin has had all along and is said out loud in the file rather than implied. **The floor, honestly**: row 8 wants a population close rate with automatic lowering, there is no dashboard, so what shipped is the per-user half (a fortnight of your own days under 70% loosens the line a step at the next Monday, clamped at both ends) and the population half is a script and a commit rather than a runtime. **And a ring that buzzed on arrival**: `Ring.tsx`'s comment said the closure buzz fires on the EDGE and the code did the opposite, seeding its ref to `false`, which is a claim about a ring nobody had seen. It never fired in practice only because the one caller passes a fraction that reaches 1 on a perfect recording alone; the challenge ring mounts full on every evening it was closed.

282. 2026-09-14 · **What the challenge did NOT take from the build list** · so the next job starts from a list rather than a re-read. **Row 10, resumption**: a recording abandoned past ~30 seconds kept and offered as a one-tap card on Today until midnight, discarded silently under ten. Not built; the recorder has no abandon path that keeps anything. **Row 11, reward the comeback**: the biggest coin grant and the biggest moment in the whole loop on the session AFTER a miss, named ("back on the board"), with the 30-in-a-row milestone getting less.
