283. 2026-09-14 · **About is the landing page and it is reachable from one place, which is correct** · the question was whether `/about` is relevant. It is the acquisition surface: the symptom-first headline, the daily-loop card, the Yoodli positioning, the camera-privacy section and the refuses-to-be list all live there, and `app/manifest.ts` deliberately opens the installed app on `/` instead, so an installed user meets it only on purpose. Inside the product it is one row in Settings ("What Ethos is"), which is the right weight for a page that exists to convert somebody who has not started. The one link that was missing is now on the account screen (#277), where somebody deciding whether to sign up is the one person in the app who wants to read what the product is. Also fixed while in there: the file's own metadata comment explained a word choice with "practice says gym rather than one-off", which is CLAUDE.md's one banned word, in the source of the page that sells the thing.

284. 2026-09-15 · **Light is the app, and the OS stopped getting a vote** · the theme had three values and the third, "System", was the default, so a phone on auto-dark decided what Ethos looked like. Every screen in `docs/look/`, every reference read, the whole warm cream ground in `STATE.md`'s visual system: that is the room the product was designed in, and most people were never opening it. Dark is untouched and still complete; it is now a switch you throw on a device rather than something inferred from it. Four places carried the old shape and all four had to move together: `lib/prefs.ts` (two values, `light` the default, and a reader that turns a stored `"system"` into light rather than garbage, because that word is in real browsers today), `components/Theme.tsx` (the boot script stops asking `prefers-color-scheme`, and `ThemeSync` stops listening for it; the motion listener stays, because #221's rule is that movement is a request the OS is allowed to make and colour is not), `app/settings/page.tsx` (two chips), and `app/layout.tsx`, which now sends `data-theme="light"` from the server so the default is right in the first byte and the inline script only has to override for a device that chose dark. **Two things that only showed up once the OS lost the vote.** `color-scheme` was never declared, which did not matter while the theme followed the OS and matters immediately now: a phone on auto-dark would have painted dark scrollbars, a dark caret and dark pickers onto the cream page, so `:root` says `light` and the dark block says `dark`. And the Settings chips were `flex-1`, which was fine at three and at two made two half-width buttons that read as a segmented control, a shape this app does not own: they size to their words now, like the reminder chips directly above them. `lib/theme.test.ts` asserts the default, the legacy read, the absent media query, the boot script's two outcomes (including that motion still hears the OS), the two chips, both `color-scheme` values and the server-sent attribute. `public/sw.js` bumped to v10 so the cached shell, which carries the old inline boot script, is replaced rather than served offline.

285. 2026-09-15 · **Speed Insights, because DESIGN.md makes a claim that needs field numbers** · "Speed is the loudest signal. Under 100ms perceived in the recording loop" is the one line in `DESIGN.md` that is a measurement rather than a taste, and nothing was measuring it. `@vercel/speed-insights` in `app/layout.tsx` puts real-user LCP, INP and CLS behind it. Two notes on the install. npm cannot resolve the package from scratch: its OPTIONAL peer on `@sveltejs/kit` drags in a Svelte vite plugin wanting vite 8 against vitest's vite 7, so the first install needs `--legacy-peer-deps`; the lockfile it writes is one clean entry with no Svelte in it, and `npm ci` and a clean `npm install` both work without the flag afterwards, which is what CI and Vercel run. And `public/sw.js` now skips `/_vercel/`: its asset branch is cache-first on anything ending `.js`, so the telemetry script would have outlived every deploy that fixed it, and a missed measurement offline is the right failure.

286. 2026-09-15 · **The OS gets its vote back: #284 reverted on Timothy's call** · #284 argued that light is the room the product was designed in and that a phone on auto-dark should not decide what Ethos looks like. Timothy's call is the other way and it outranks the entry, so the setting is three values again with `system` the default. The argument #284 was answering does not go away, it just loses: a device on auto-dark has already answered the question, and asking it again is the app overriding a preference the user set once for everything they own. **What came back**: `lib/prefs.ts` (three values, `system` default), `components/Theme.tsx` (the boot script and `applyTheme` resolve `system`, and `ThemeSync` listens for `prefers-color-scheme` again so a phone that flips at sunset moves with it), `app/settings/page.tsx` (three chips at `flex-1`, which is the shape that shipped for months and whose "reads as a segmented control" problem was specific to two). `app/layout.tsx` drops the server-sent `data-theme="light"`: with the OS back in the answer only the browser knows it, and a guess there is a wrong first paint for everyone it does not match. **What stayed, because it was never about the default**: `color-scheme` on `:root` and the dark block, which follows the RESOLVED attribute and is therefore correct under `system` too, and the service worker's `/_vercel/` skip. Cache bumped to v11, because the inline boot script changed again. **The one thing this cannot undo.** #284 coerced a stored `"system"` to light on READ, and `writePrefs` spreads `readPrefs()`, so any device that changed any other setting during the roughly three hours light was live, a reminder hour, haptics, sound, had `theme: "light"` written into its storage as a side effect. Those are indistinguishable from a real choice now and will stay on light until the person picks System. The fix is not a migration: a migration here would overwrite the genuine choices made in the same window, which is the worse error. `readTheme` reads an unrecognised value as `system` rather than as light, so the one group that does recover is anyone whose storage holds neither word. **The lesson worth keeping**: a read-time coercion is not a safe way to retire a stored value, because a write path that reads the whole object first turns every coercion into a migration nobody chose. `lib/theme.test.ts` now asserts both resolvers against the same cases (stored beats OS, `system` resolves rather than stamping the word `system` onto an attribute no CSS rule matches, junk falls back to `system`, motion still hears the OS) and that the boot script's rule is character-identical to the one in `lib/prefs.ts`, since the two are the same rule written twice in two languages.

287. 2026-09-16 · **Graded against the seven design principles: eight findings, six fixed, two withdrawn on measurement** · Timothy asked how well alignment, balance, contrast, space, text formatting, usability and navigation are implemented. The answer is that they are encoded rather than applied (one rhythm, text roles pinned to AA, one tab list, a test for the way off every bare screen), graded 8/10, with eight concrete findings. Fixed: (1) the five trait cards on Today each said "scale provisional", one disclaimer printed five times on the first screen, and now print the measurement alone while the dashed ring (#264) and the strip's caption carry the estimate; (2) `challengeLine` said "1.8 today, over 1.8." when today formatted the same as the line, because equality closes (`todayS >= lineS`) and the verb did not know it, so it says "on the line", asserted in the test; (3) the results walk's first step left a third of a phone screen empty between the coach bubble and the bottom-anchored button, and now centres in the space the walk gives it, as the timer does on the recording screen; (4) the log truncated "Punctuate with si…" in a 134px column, and a lesson name now wraps to two lines; (5) `sand` measured 1.11:1 on the ground, so the six zero bars on You read as blank rows, and both themes step one notch firmer (#e5d5b2 light at 1.22:1, #443627 dark at 1.57:1) with the dashed trough still firmer; (6) the tab bar's five labels move from the 10px `.label-micro` to the 11px `.label-data`, since they are the most-read small type in the app and 10 is iOS's floor. Also the two targets under 44px the sweep found on You, the Settings link at 53×20 and Edit at 24 wide, which take the margin trick Today's text links use. Withdrawn: the lesson cards' "0 of 3" counts were called misaligned when one title wraps, but a 1:1 clip shows both pinned to the card foot at the same y, an artefact of reading a 5800px page at a fifth of its size; and Tools was called 40% empty at phone height, but measured, the last row ends 82px above the tab bar with the screen 89% filled. Two rules for the loop from that: grade from a clip at 1:1, never a downsampled full page, and measure a gap before calling it one. Before and after in `docs/look/principles/`.

288. 2026-09-16 · **The introduction, rebuilt against Duolingo's, in five steps, and what was not copied** · Timothy recorded Duolingo's introduction (docs/refs/duolingo-onboarding, the first entry in the refs folder) and said what he could not explain: simple, empty, brings your attention, makes him want to do the lesson. Named, the mechanics are: the mascot is the speaker and there are no headings; the bubble's words land before he moves; nothing else is on the page; the button is grey until you answer; he replies on the same screen in his own voice; options are objects with a glyph; one bar, no count; a beat between questions. He said go, in order. (1) Demos speaks: the intro lines and the questions sit in a speech bubble with a tail on him (`components/SpeechBubble.tsx`, raised paper on a card-edge hairline rather than the results screen's terracotta wash, because the chosen answer takes that wash and his voice and your tap should not share a colour), above him on the intros and beside him on the questions, through a `speech` prop on `LessonScreen` so the lesson screens are untouched. The words land one at a time over about 220ms after the screen's own slide (`components/Says.tsx`, word spans at staggered delays, the whole line in the DOM from the first frame so nothing under it moves) and he nods once after the last word (`greetAfterMs` on `DemosArt`, which also drops its outer margin and cleans the nod class off at animation end). A reply replaces the question in the bubble, the reference's own move, with the question kept as a hidden heading. Poses are served unoptimised and the next screen's pose is warmed on each step, because the strip showed him landing 300ms after his own words. (2) Next waits for an answer on every question, the name and the optional ones included, and lights terracotta the moment one exists; Skip stays under it as the way past, so nothing became mandatory. The old rule, essential questions hold and optional ones do not, was two rules for one button, and the `essential` flag is gone. (3) The seven-segment bar and "2 of 7" become one continuous bar (`role="progressbar"`, the width transitioning because the element persists across questions) and five of the seven sublines are cut: "Pick the closest.", "Roughly." and "Pick when you'd actually do it." were instructions the rows already give, "It changes the prompts you get, nothing else." was a design decision justified to the user, "Optional." is what Skip says. "I'm Demos, by the way." stays because it is where he is introduced, and "Up to three." because the pains are the one multi-choice in a list that looks like every single-choice. (4) Answers are 56px objects with a glyph at the left and the chosen one lit in terracotta, an edge on the wash with a ring inside it, instead of #206's inverted ink block, which was the heaviest thing on the screen and marked the answer rather than the action. Eight glyphs join `Icon.tsx` for the words the set had nothing for (trail, flat, paragraph, board, case, people, globe, and signal bars lit to a level); the age bands take their number as the mark; the hour rows are two columns, the word and the time, which is the reference's own goal screen. (5) One beat before the hour: Demos alone with "Practice with a time happens. Sixty seconds, at an hour you'd keep." It is not a question, the bar does not move for it, and it promises nothing the walk cannot do, since no permission is asked here; Timothy said no restrictions on this pass, and the reminder vocabulary was kept off the screen anyway because a promised reminder before permission exists is a lie, not a restriction. Not copied: the voice ("Let's get this party started!" is the register COPY-RULES bans), the 3D bottom-edge buttons, the glossy bar, hearts. Before and after through all twelve screens in `docs/look/duo/`; `scripts/check-onboarding.mjs` walks the beat and asserts the new button rule and the bar, 47/48 with the one red that predates #284.

306. 2026-09-25 · **The spin is a slot reel** · "Longer spin on spin". Overrides #278's "fast, on purpose". The topic roulette and the boss wheel share `components/Reel.tsx`, now 2.2s through eighteen candidates and the landing: steady braking with the last cell crossing at 90% of travel, ticks that space out as it slows, a moving 0.04-cell bounce past the landing rather than a hold, then a pop on the landed prompt. The path is a function of time (`spinAt` in `lib/motion.ts`) sampled into WAAPI keyframes, because one cubic-bezier cannot both brake late and arrive moving. `SPIN` is the one motion allowed past `DURATION.celebrate`, pinned by `lib/motion.test.ts`. The reel never flashes the prompt it is leaving or the one it lands on mid-spin.

307. 2026-09-25 · **Google can always be tapped again** · "After you click out you can't click back into sign in with google." The button set `busy` and only an error gave it back, so a back from Google (bfcache), a dismissed OAuth sheet in the installed app, or a tab switch left it dead, and on /signup the email form died with it. `lib/use-oauth-return.ts` owns the pending state for /signup, /signin and the introduction's account ask: a persisted pageshow, a return to visible, focus, or 5s gives it back, it never touches the email form, and pending is held ("Opening Google…") rather than greyed. `/auth/callback` reads OAuth errors from the query and the hash, so a cancel says "Google sign-in didn't finish." with Try again back to where the attempt started (`ethos.oauth` in sessionStorage, same-origin only), and it waits for the session instead of a fixed 400ms that could call a slow success a used link. A finished introduction reopens on its account ask when a Google attempt from it came back without an account. Gate: `scripts/check-google-return.mjs`.

316. 2026-09-26 · **Demos moves: idle clips over the stills** · Timothy: "do the demos idle animations", offered after the 3D pass. A raster swaying on a CSS loop reads as a sticker being moved; the 3D Demos now blinks, waves, talks, bounces, glances at the thing in his paws and tilts his head. Seven clips (wave, speaking, celebrate, clipboard, clock, hello, listening), one per pose that stands large on a screen (the intro stages and the beat, the plan and the account ask, the boss card, the You header, the topic card before a recording, the scoring wait); the 84px question avatars keep their CSS loops, because a clip there is bytes nobody sees move. Each is a Kling v3.0 render with the shipped 3D still as both its first and last frame, so a clip starts and ends on the still already on screen: the swap is invisible and a clip that stops holds the still (Seedance 1.5 was tried first and swapped which arm waved mid-clip). Transparency is the hard part: no one video format carries alpha in every browser, so each clip stacks its colour (premultiplied) over its alpha as grey in an ordinary video, VP9 WebM first and H.264 MP4 for Safari, 100 to 260KB each, and `components/DemosClip.tsx` rejoins them in a small WebGL shader. It only ever adds: the still renders first from the server, the clip fades in on the first frame it actually draws, and no WebGL, a codec the browser lacks, iOS Low Power Mode refusing autoplay or a lost context all leave the still where it was; reduced motion and Save-Data never fetch it. He plays, rests 2.6 to 5.2s (drawn afresh each rest), and plays again, first after his words land (#288), paused off screen and in a hidden tab. The service worker leaves `/idle/` to the browser, because a video is fetched in ranges and a cache cannot hold a 206. `scripts/make-idle-clips.py` rebuilds the set from the recorded sources; `lib/idle-clips.test.ts` holds every file to existing and under 280KB (the celebration's bounce sets the ceiling). Cutting him out is the other hard part: rembg `isnet-general-use`, except the celebration, whose bounce blurs his tail into something isnet reads as half background (a grey ghost of a tail on the dark stage), cut with `birefnet-general-lite`; and every frame is made solid more than four pixels inside his outline, from the frame's own colour, so a blinking eyelid the matte half-dropped cannot show the stage through his face. `scripts/check-motion.mjs` now reads the Index the moment the results land: software WebGL drawing the scoring clip in headless Chromium slowed the old read (taken after awaiting the streak dialog) past the end of the count. 52 Higgsfield credits, including two test renders and one re-render (the first listening clip took the paw off his ear).

(2) Sheets: 1:1 drag with ten pixels of hysteresis, rubber band upward, velocity from the last 100ms, dismiss decided by the projected resting point, and every exit leaving from the live position (it used to jump to the top and replay a keyframe); the scrim frosts as well as dims. 377. 2026-10-25 · **Premium is one plan whose price drops every month you stay (supersedes #197)** · Timothy's call ("cheaper with time... if they stop subbing and sub back in they have to start from the start"), with his three answers: the middle ladder, A$12.99, 11.99, 10.99, 9.99, 8.99, 7.99, then A$6.99 from month 7 on (`lib/pricing.ts`, an unbroken first year A$104.88); the annual plan is dropped, so the ladder is the only price; and only a plan that ENDS resets it (a cancel taking effect, or Stripe's retries running out), while a declined card that Stripe is retrying keeps its place and its Premium. From #197 the disciplines that survive: money buys no score, no lifetime, no weekly, no decoy, no time-limited trial. What does not: "annual pushed hard" and "price tests upward only". The interface never says "addictive": the framing is the price dropping every month you stay, and the reset rule is said once per surface as a fact. Australian consumer law and the subscription-trap rules set the floor: the whole ladder, the renewal and the reset rule are on the paywall, in Checkout's submit text and in Terms before anyone pays, and cancelling takes as many taps as starting.

378. 2026-10-25 · **The ladder is seven Stripe prices and one step after each paid month, never a Subscription Schedule** · One product, seven monthly AUD prices (GST inclusive, lookup keys `ethos_ladder_m1` to `m7`); Checkout starts every plan on month 1, and after each paid month the server moves the plan's single item to the next rung with proration "none", so the next renewal charges the next rung. A schedule would have committed every price up front, but a schedule-managed subscription cannot have its cancellation changed directly and the customer portal will not manage it, which would have made cancelling harder than starting. A month is a paid invoice on the current plan whose reason is `subscription_create` or `subscription_cycle`; the price never steps back up (paid months only grow, so two crossing syncs cannot raise it); a plan on a price that is not a rung is never touched; and every price is verified against the ladder before it is charged (amount, AUD only, monthly), failing closed. Adaptive pricing is off, so everyone pays the A$ the paywall shows. `scripts/stripe-setup.mjs` builds the product, the prices, the portal and the webhook in one idempotent run.

379. 2026-10-25 · **Stripe is the record; one sync function writes the copy** · `syncCustomer` (`lib/billing.ts`) is the only writer of the `billing` table (migration 0012) and of a paid plan's Premium. Every webhook event, the return from Checkout and the sync route call it, and it reads Stripe's current state rather than trusting an event's snapshot, so delivery order and duplicates do not matter. After writing it reads Stripe again and runs once more if anything moved (three passes, then the webhook answers 500 and Stripe retries), which closes the last-writer-wins race two concurrent syncs had, without a lock column. Premium on a paid plan carries `premium_until` at the period end plus three days, a backstop that runs out if the "ended" webhook is ever lost. A premium given by invite code (`premium_source = 'code'`) is never touched by billing. The client reads the copy through `fetchBilling` and calls sync once on a return, never polling it.

380. 2026-10-25 · **The browser can no longer write its own entitlement** · 0003's "own profile update" policy let any signed-in session set `profiles.premium` to true from the console: harmless while Premium came from an invite, not once it costs money. Migration 0012's trigger keeps `premium`, `premium_until` and `premium_source` at the server's values for any `anon` or `authenticated` session, and 0013 extends the same guard to the judged-analysis meter's columns (the server review found a browser could reset its own meter and get unlimited judged reads, the metered thing Premium sells). Names and poses still save. Checked on a scratch Postgres 16: a browser session cannot flip either, the service role still can.

381. 2026-10-25 · **A paid plan needs a saved account** · Anonymous-first sessions keep everything else, but checkout answers 403 `needsAccount`, and the paywall turns its tap into "Create an account", returning to the sheet after sign-up or sign-in (`next=` with `paywall=1`). Why: a plan bought on an anonymous session lives on one browser's storage, and losing the device would lose a thing someone pays for every month. The user id survives the upgrade, so the recordings come along (#15's no-migration property).

382. 2026-10-25 · **What each Stripe status means in Ethos** · active and trialing are Premium; past_due is Premium while the card is retried and holds the rung; unpaid is a live plan with Premium off; incomplete is a first payment still going through (checkout answers "manage", never a second plan); canceled and incomplete_expired have ended, and a new plan starts at month 1. A cancel date further out than this period still reads as renewing until that date. When retries run out Stripe leaves the last invoice open and payable, so the sync voids it: nobody can pay for a month with no Premium.

383. 2026-10-25 · **One account, one plan: duplicates are stopped at Checkout** · Found independently by all three reviewers of the server's triple check: two Checkout pages open at once (a second tab, a phone then a laptop) could each become a plan, both charging A$12.99 every month. Checkout now expires every older open session for the customer, checks Stripe again for a plan, and gives the new session 35 minutes (Stripe's minimum plus latency) instead of a day; an in-app cancel ends every live plan on the account, not just the one shown; and the setup checklist turns on Stripe's own one-subscription limit. The sync still never cancels a duplicate by itself, so a plan is never ended by a guess.

384. 2026-10-25 · **Deleting an account ends its billing first** · Before anything is deleted, `/api/account` closes the customer's open Checkout pages, cancels every plan that has not ended (immediately, no refund) and voids their unpaid invoices; with no Stripe key it refuses while a plan may still be live. A plan the webhook later finds for a user who no longer exists (a Checkout paid in the gap) is cancelled if it is on the ladder's product and logged for a manual refund. A customer the key cannot see (test data under a live key) is forgotten only on a live key or when Stripe says it was deleted, so a Preview pointed at the production database can never wipe a live plan.

385. 2026-10-25 · **The ladder is drawn as a staircase with real months, after two directions were built and judged** · Timothy: "design the fuck out of this, take inspiration from the top 10 Apple Design Awards, make it look finished". The research pass picked ten winners and finalists for this job (Flighty, Duolingo, Any Distance, Gentler Streak, (Not Boring) Habits, Halide, Tide Guide, Crouton, Bears Gratitude, Copilot Money), and three directions were built as workbench pages (`app/workbench/ladder-a`, `-b`; shots in `docs/look/ladder-directions/`). B, the staircase, won the object: seven steps measured from zero in the answer rows' grammar (2px edge, 4px lip), the paid months pressed flat, the current one lit with the Paw standing on it, the floor bleeding off the sheet's edge to say "forever" without a word. A, the timeline, gave it three things: real calendar months under the steps (a price never appears without its date, Flighty and Copilot), the readout that rolls only the digits that change as you scrub (Halide, Tide Guide), and the manage sheet's plain inset group (Crouton). Plum extends to a third Premium surface, the price-drop sheet, because that is the tier speaking; You's card, the manage sheet and Settings stay neutral, because managing a plan is a utility. Demos appears at the two moments only, the return from Checkout and the price drop (Bears Gratitude). The spec is `docs/ladder-spec.md`.

386. 2026-10-25 · **Cancel is in the app, two taps, and Keep my plan undoes it** · The manage sheet's Cancel plan turns the group into a confirm stating the facts (Premium runs to the end of the paid month; if the plan ends, the next one starts at A$12.99) with Keep my plan beside the terracotta Cancel plan, so cancelling takes as many taps as starting and is never hidden, greyed or shamed. An ending plan's one tap is Keep my plan, which keeps its place on the ladder. Stripe's portal is only for the card and receipts. The manage sheet's active state has no terracotta at all: nothing there is the next step, so "one tap per screen" reads as at most one.

387. 2026-10-25 · **The price drop is celebrated once, on this device, from the month it was last seen** · `ethos.ladder.seen` holds the last month this device celebrated. First sight stores silently (no celebration for a month nobody watched arrive), the return from Checkout stores month 1, a new plan whose month went down stores silently, and a month above the stored one opens the sheet over Today or You, whichever is first. A blank or unreadable value never reads as month 0.

388. 2026-10-25 · **A spring interrupted mid-flight keeps its speed** · From Justin Sun's reel on why iOS feels nice (Timothy shared it): iOS's rubber band is a hyperbola with a ceiling, `(1 − 1/(c·x/d + 1))·d` with c = 0.55, and its animations are springs that keep their velocity when grabbed halfway, where a timer animation keeps the position and throws the speed away. The first was already true here: `rubberband()` (#321) is the same expression rearranged, with the same 0.55. The second was true only for a finger's release. `lib/spring.ts` now reads a running spring's speed analytically (`springVelocity`, `springStateAt`), finds the instant on its curve that matches where the element actually is (`velocityAt`, exact even when the caller has already cancelled it or the animation started a frame late), and `animateSpring` carries that speed into the new target whenever no finger supplied one; a new animation also starts on the frame it is asked for, not the next one, which removed a one-to-two-frame hold at every release and retarget. The tab bar's well now travels this way on a tap (two fast taps swing through instead of stopping dead; measured at 390), and the sheets and the Paw follow.

389. 2026-10-25 · **What the interface's triple check changed** · Four independent reviews (design, behaviour and accessibility, copy and law, a live browser run) and a skeptic per finding confirmed about forty defects; all are fixed with a test or a shot. The ones that would have hurt: "make an account and you'll come straight back here" was false for sign-up and Google (every path now carries `next`); six pages that open the paywall never reopened it (every host mounts `usePaywallReturn`, asserted in `lib/paywall-hosts.test.ts`); "Not yet" while Checkout was being asked still sent the browser to Stripe; at 320 the invite form widened the sheet past the screen; cancelling an unpaid plan promised "Premium runs to…" while Premium was off and offered Cancel forever. Calls made on the way: at 320 the step faces stay uniform at 14/8 rather than hiding the two-digit ones (the spec is amended); on a past-due plan the Paw stays on the last paid month and the words name the month being retried; an unpaid plan's stair counts its steps rather than dating them, since Stripe keeps rolling unpaid periods; Settings reserves the Manage plan row only on a device that last saw a plan (`ethos.plan.row`); dark's neutral paid steps are white at 8%; `SpeechBubble` gains a plum material for the price-drop sheet; a held plan's paywall tap becomes Update card; a finger on the stair selects on lift or after 8px sideways, so a vertical scroll that starts on it selects nothing; deleting an account with a live plan says, before the confirm, that it ends now with no refund for the days left. Left for the server: cancelling a past-due plan could end it at once and void the open invoice, and a full delete would also delete the Stripe customer (Privacy says it stays).

390. 2026-10-25 · **React #418 had a root cause, and it was the root layout** · The intermittent hydration error STATE carried since #341 (about one cold load in a hundred, on /, /you, /settings and /games) was not in any page. With React's hydration patched to say where it failed: React hydrates inside a transition, and an element still waiting on a JavaScript chunk is retried from the wrong place. Two elements in the root layout waited. `themeBootScript` was imported into the server layout from `components/Theme.tsx`, a client file, so the first `<script>` in `<head>` waited on that file's chunk, and retrying the head left `<body>` hydrating against `<meta charset>`. And `PageTransition`'s div held Next's router element directly, which waits on the error and not-found chunks. The script now lives in a plain module (`lib/theme-boot.ts`) and the page sits under a keyed fragment, with the DOM unchanged. Before: 9 in about 900 loads. After: 0 in 960, plus all six gates on the production build.

391. 2026-10-26 · **The five tabs are one app: Practice's grammar is the house grammar** · A first-time tester went through the tabs in dark on an iPhone (`docs/refs/feedback/2026-10-25-dark-tabs.md`). Her read:
- "None of the pages match really."
- On Today: "everything competes", "you don't know this is the main thing".
- On Lessons: "the colors are too bright", "lessons look really weird".
- On You: "nothing matches this one because of the panda".
- On Practice: "practice is good".

Timothy said to take it seriously, and the shots agreed. Each tab had its own colour world:
- Today: an amber card, a blue ring and five tones.
- Lessons: fifteen saturated glossy squares and tone-coloured heads.
- Log: an olive card and a maroon callout.
- You: a grey card with Demos on a blue coin.

Practice, the one she liked, had one coloured stage and nothing else coloured. So:
- **One stage.** Every tab now opens on one sky stage (`HeroStage`) holding its one hero, and its one tap stands under it.
- **Colour under the stage.** Under the stage nothing has a coloured fill. A trait's colour is an 8px square, rings are stone, and dark mutes the tones (while keeping Pausing and Variety apart).
- **Progress.** One rule: sage on sand off the stage, the stage's ink on it, and grey only for the trough.
- **Lessons.** The fifteen objects are cut out of their gradient squares (`scripts/key-lesson-art.py`, service worker cache v16) and stand on one neutral tile. Up next stands its object on the stage like a figure.
- **Today.** Its topic sets at 20/800 so a typical topic holds three lines.

Principles bent, and why:
- **Sage leaves the Log's score card** (#362): the stage is the hero's material everywhere, and sage still marks the stars, the streak and earned progress.
- **Demos stands on Today's and You's stages as on Practice's**: every stage wants a figure at its right. That stretches "Demos at moments", so only one Demos is ever on screen, and the stage's Demos steps off while the price drop shows.

Measured by the pass:
- Today's distinct hues went from 6 to 3, and coloured pixels under the stage from 1.8% to 0.2%.
- Lessons' coloured area under the stage went from 7.2% to 2.0%.

An independent critic marked each of her sentences resolved or not with screenshots. Its sixteen defects were fixed in a second round. The gates were all green on the production build: tsc, 2201 tests, and the six browser checks at their counts. Before and after sheets: `docs/look/cohesion/contact-dark.png`, `contact-light.png`.

392. 2026-10-27 · **A bug pass: the shop's prices, a leaking view, the declined cancel, the Stripe customer, and 37 bugs from a hunt** · Timothy: "lets do some bug fixes".
- **Known items, each fixed test first:**
  - The shop took its price from the caller, so a direct call bought a freeze or a pose for one coin, and freezes protect streaks. The price now comes from a server catalog (`shop_prices`, migration 0015, kept equal to `lib/shop.ts` by a drift test). A spend at the wrong price is refused, and only a spend at the catalog price proves ownership.
  - `purchased_freezes` showed every account's id to anyone. It now runs as the reader, so it returns only their own row. It is unused and kept.
  - Cancelling a declined plan now ends it at once and voids the charge Stripe was retrying, and the person is told that before they confirm.
  - Deleting an account now deletes the Stripe customer too, with its email and saved card. Privacy says so.
- **The hunt:** three hunters read and drove the core loop, the data and sync, and the screen states. A skeptic upheld 37 of 38 findings, and each was fixed test first. The worst:
  - Stop could hang the scoring screen forever.
  - Go again did nothing.
  - A second capture could leave the mic recording.
  - Outbox recordings were dated on upload day and had no owner. They now carry when they were spoken, clamped to the last 72 hours, and who made them.
  - The streak, the longest streak and the day count were computed from only the newest 90 recordings.
  - The database capped held freezes at 2 while the code allowed 3.
  - A sent-twice recording was stored twice. Migration 0016 keys reps on a client id.
  - The challenge week's coin was judged on UTC days (migration 0018).
  - Push reminders drifted an hour across daylight saving (migration 0019 stores the zone).
  - Several reads drew a failure as zero.
- **Gates:** 2355 tests, the production build, and the six browser gates at their counts. Two gate fixes landed on the way: route files may only export handlers, now asserted in `lib/route-exports.test.ts`; and Demos's idle clip rests while a sheet covers him, which had made a long drag read as slow.
- **Migrations:** 0015, 0016, 0018 and 0019 are applied to production. 0017 (`refund_rate_limit`) is not, because the connector stalls on its `delete` for a confirmation that never arrives. Until it is applied, a transcription that fails costs its rate-limit slot, which the code tolerates.

394. 2026-10-29 · **Timothy's four notes: the introduction keeps the chop only, the paywall loses the Paw and swipes away from anywhere, the Log gets a Go per lesson** · His words: "the image like animates by moving the png around and then a video animation plays after that image chop animation. Looks weird. Lets js have the chopping image animation. Lets remove the paw from the paywall. I cant reliably scroll down the paywall, to move it away. in log remove start. and js add a go button to each waiting to be logged, make it match tho."
- **The introduction:** every Demos on `/welcome` goes through `IntroDemos`, which never mounts the idle clip, so he pops in and stays the picture. Now every sheet listens to `touchmove` itself and holds the finger from its first downward move unless the content under it is mid-scroll (`lib/sheet-gesture.ts`); a finger on the stair is shared with the sheet and a mouse stays the stair's; the grabber, mark and headline sit in a head that never scrolls on screens 640px or taller; a sheet closes when thrown past half its height or pulled three quarters of the way to the edge, so a thumb resting on Start can close it; a thumb's swipe arcs, so a first move that leans sideways still belongs to the sheet unless it starts on the paywall's slider, and the pull is measured from the press; a sheet once held never replays its rise, which had blinked it off screen on a spring home. A touch matrix of real CDP touches at 390x844, 375x667 and 320x568 closes from every region with room to swipe. Not yet tested on a real iPhone.

All three are the secondary button: three terracotta taps would be three primaries, and the populated Log has none either, so this breaks principle 2 in the letter only. 397. 2026-10-30 · **Tester mode for the first cohort, and the ship that carried it** · Timothy is recruiting about ten friends for 14 days from Monday 2 November, A$35 each on finishing, to find out whether the scores are right. His words: "build the match button and consent screen... On onboarding add a tester? and code is 1234 and add the tester features there. make sure supabase isolates these peoples data."
- **The door:** "Tester code" on the introduction's first screen and in Settings; four digits, checked only on the server (`app/api/tester`, `TESTER_CODE` or "1234", constant time, the redeem route's two rate limits). The first call checks the code and stores nothing; the second carries the consent version and, with a session, writes the `testers` row with the consent in one upsert. Backing out of consent leaves nothing.
- **The code grants nothing paid.** 1234 is the first code anyone guesses and Premium is what Ethos sells; a tester Timothy wants on Premium gets the invite code as well.
- **The match check:** testers only, on results, "Did this score match how it felt?" Yes / Roughly / No, one tap, changeable, an optional "What felt off?" note, stored with the scores shown and keyed by the outbox client id (results can show before the upload lands).
- **Isolation (0020):** `testers` (select own only, no client writes) and `tester_feedback` (own rows only; insert and update only while a testers row exists), both cascading with the account; the report lives in `tester_admin`, a schema the API never exposes, closed to anon and authenticated. Applied to production and proven there in a transaction that rolled itself back: a tester writes only their own answers; writing as another user, a non-tester writing, a self-made tester, and anon or authenticated reading the report are all refused; each user sees only their own rows; removing an account takes its tester rows.
- **What shipped to main (2fbc253):** everything since 8e43f16 (the home, Timothy's four notes, the pose on You, the anti-slop skill, tester mode) except the scrolled-sheet hand-off: its review found three high findings still being fixed, so main carries the sheet code from 58ba383 until it passes. On the ship tree: tsc, 2587 tests, the production build, and the six browser gates at 22, 81, 12, 25, 20 and 45.
- **Next for testers:** Timothy's full consent list (device, usage, connection and errors as well), collecting those for testers only, the match answers as a direction (too low, about right, too high) with one tap before the score shows, and A$35 in the guide.

401. 2026-10-31 · **Ethos goes native; the ladder stays on the web** · Timothy: "We are not staying web only." This replaces the 30 October advice to stay a web app. In the App Store build a subscription bought inside the app must go through Apple's in-app purchase (Guideline 3.1.1), and Apple's subscriptions cannot step a price down every month without the user buying a new product, so the ladder (#377) cannot run there. The plan: the app sells one fixed monthly price through in-app purchase, the ladder's first rung (A$12.99), so the web is never dearer; speakethos.com keeps the ladder through Stripe; Premium belongs to the account, so a plan bought on the web works in the app (Guideline 3.1.3(b), which also requires the in-app purchase to exist). Timothy's call is to send people to the web for the cheaper price. Outside the app (Instagram, email, the site) that is always allowed. Inside the iOS app it waits for the Federal Court's order of 22 October 2026 to take effect in Australia (by 1 March 2027; Apple is appealing). Nothing is built yet: the native wrapper, in-app purchase and the shared entitlement are the paywall step's work when native starts.

402. 2026-10-31 · **Tester mode is on Today** · Timothy, 31 Oct: "Can u make tester more like itsvthere more noticeable", and of the DM, "make sure it says to send me the tester number!!". Before this a tester could go a day without seeing anything that said they were testing but the two questions around a recording, and the tester number sat three levels deep (Settings, About, Testing Ethos). Now, for a device holding the tester hint: Today's eyebrow reads "Test day 2 of 14" with the mark's flask in mint ink, in the date's place during the test (it breaks #393's date over the greeting for 14 days, for testers only; on a short phone the tile is under the nav when Today opens, so the eyebrow carries the test there), and a tile under the practice card holds a mint Tester chip with a flask and the tester number, then one ask, then "1 of 14 days spoken" and the 14 days as a road (sage spoken since joining, today ringed in ink, sand for a day gone by, outlined ahead, faded before they joined) with 30 Oct and 12 Nov at its ends. The ask sits right under the number, above the count: on an iPhone opened from the home screen the floating nav stands 34px higher (the home indicator's inset) and the tile's last row was under it, and "Send Timothy your tester number." is the one line that must never be. The ask, the first that applies: Testing is paused with Agree again (in the tile's grammar, ink line and grey key, since it outranks the rest); Send Timothy your tester number with Send (the share sheet with the number typed in, Copy where there is none) until it is sent from that phone; Speak today, then answer both questions; Did 665 feel right? with Answer, opening the Log's question (`?from=today#match`: its way back is Today, and it lands at the screen's foot with the answers on screen); or Day 2 is done. From 13 to 19 November the road stands still and the ask says the test is over (or Send, while unsent); from 20 November it is gone. The same chip sits over "How did that feel?" and beside "Did 665 feel right?", and Settings' row reads "Day 2 of 14", then "Ended". One fixed height (208px) in every state, held from the server's first paint by a head script (`lib/tester-boot.ts`) so a reload moves nothing; the bento's flat surface step under the one lifted card, no terracotta, no Demos, nothing on Lessons, Practice, the Log list or You; a non-tester's Today is pixel-identical and reads nothing new, and a tester's testers row is read once per page life, shared by the layer and the tile. The day is read again whenever the app comes back and once a minute, so a phone left on Today overnight never says yesterday is done. The test's days are counted in the zone the report counts `days_spoken` in (`tester_admin.zones`, 0020): the reminders' zone on a phone whose reminders the server holds (`lib/reminders-zone.ts`, the zone it sent), Melbourne's (`TEST.zone`) otherwise; for a friend in Melbourne they are the same days, and a friend elsewhere without reminders sees the test day turn at Melbourne's midnight. **Collection ends with the test** (decided here, Timothy can reverse it): from 00:00 on 13 November in Melbourne (`TEST.endsAt`) a phone collects nothing (`collects`) and `app/api/tester/collect` takes nothing and tells every phone to stop, because the consent keeps what is collected "while you test" and Today and Settings say the test ended; the two questions still run, since answering is the tester's own act. From the look loop: Answer took Send's grammar (ink line, grey key); a faded day fades its fill, not the slot; ahead days are outlined at 1.5px, since 1px vanished in dark; a history that did not load rings no day (the ring beside the count's dash read as a 0); Send's word never leaves the key (it fades from 40%), and focus moves to the next ask when the key goes. Refs: Wellspoken's week row (`docs/refs/wellspoken/04-home.png`), Duolingo's path slots (`docs/refs/duolingo/01-path.png`); the tester-presence debate, B's road with A's mark. Open for Timothy: the pay bar for friends who joined after the 30th (the guide's query counts days spoken from 30 October to 12 November, and 14 cannot be met by them; the tile states no pay rule).

He is a guest (he skipped making an account), and a guest's scoring allowance is the visitor's, 3 an hour and 5 a day, rolling, so his fifth take of the day closed scoring until the first aged out. 11 of the 18 testers are guests. `/api/analyze` now gives a tester the account's 10 an hour and 30 a day whether or not they made one (`analyzeTier`, `isTester` in `lib/rate-limit.ts`, a service-role read of `testers`); a guest who is not testing keeps the visitor's. 409. 2026-11-04 · **Guests score like accounts, and only a saved recording uses up the limit** · Timothy: "give guests a higher limit please and also if they press start again dont treat it as a saved recording that sets the limit again." Replaces #408's tester-only rule: any session, guest or account, gets the account's 10 an hour and 30 a day (`analyzeTier`); only a caller with no session at all keeps the visitor's 3 and 5. And a slot is kept only when the call ends with a new stored recording: `/api/analyze` wraps its handler so that anything else (Whisper down, a take the mic barely heard, a resend of a recording already stored, a failed save, a crash) gives the slot back in one place. A file Whisper refuses still keeps its slot, so junk uploads cost one each.

410. 2026-11-04 · **An account comes before the first recording, guests included** · Timothy: "in onboarding they have to make an account", before the first recording, and existing guests too (11 of the 18 testers). This reverses #15 and #134's anonymous-first rule and /about's "No signup until you've spoken" (now "One quick account, then you're speaking."). The introduction's account ask ("Keep this.") loses its "Not now": Google or email, and both come back to the first recording (Google's `next`, the email door's `?next=`). Everywhere else, `AccountGate` (mounted in the layout, reading the session from the device, no network) sends a guest, or a device with no session that has been through the introduction, to `/signup?next=<where they were>`; open without an account are the introduction, the auth pages, the tester door and consent, about, privacy and terms (`lib/account-gate.ts`). A guest whose email link is on its way is let through, since the account exists once they open it. Both sign-up paths keep the guest's own user id (Google links the provider to the anonymous session, email attaches to it), so a guest tester's recordings and tester row stay theirs. Gates: check-onboarding 82/82 (the walk now proves a device with no account is sent to sign up and back), check-google-return 45/45, check-lessons 20/20 (its take now runs 2.5s, past #406's 1.5s floor). check-tester-collect is 35/38 with or without this change: three of its checks read the real date, and from 2 November the test is on, which they do not expect; production's own tester_events (1,165 taps and 564 screens from 16 testers in three days) show collection working.

412. 2026-11-05 · **Lite graphics, and the recording screen keeps the phone's graphics for the take** · Testers on iPhones: Nick, "sometimes shit goes black and crashes", has never saved a recording (every visit to the recording screen since 1 Nov is a one-second look); Vincent, "I couldn't do it" on day three; Alan's page died 4.5 seconds after a Video start on 3 Nov. Timothy: "We need to optimise the images and optimise for low graphics." The tester data (80 of 80 takes started under collection were Voice; the one Video start killed the page; Nick's screen went dead after a Video tap started the pose model's GPU delegate while idle; Vincent recorded only in Video, inside Instagram's in-app browser, on iOS 26.5.2 like Nick) put the weight on the camera and MediaPipe's WebGL work, with Demos's WebGL clip on the same graphics process. So: (1) **Lite graphics** (`lib/graphics.ts`): Demos stands as his still, glass goes solid, blurs and loops stop (lite is the reduced-motion stylesheet plus `data-graphics="lite"`), and Video is off. It comes on by itself after a crash (the boot script reads an "alive" mark the last page never took down, so the reload is lite from its first paint), after a lost WebGL context, and on a 2x iPhone (SE, XR, 11); Settings has the switch and says why it came on, and a choice there is kept both ways. (2) **The recording screen**: Demos is a still on the topic card and the scoring wait for everyone, and on an iPhone or iPad Video is off for now ("Video is off on iPhone for now. Voice works."): measured in Chromium, tapping Video adds about 110MB the page never gives back and a Video start takes the page from about 200 to 525MB, and 80 of 80 takes in the test were Voice. For when it comes back, the pose model there is CPU only and loads at the Rec tap, never while the screen is read. Demos's clip no longer uploads a frame from a paused or ended video, the upload WebKit loses its context over (bugs.webkit.org 261331). (3) **Instagram's browser**: the recording screen says recording is steadier in the phone's browser and how to get there; nothing is blocked. (4) **Images**: Demos's poses get a 512px cut in `public/demos-512/` for every place he stands 170pt or less (a quarter of the decoded pixels, about a third of the bytes); the 1024 set stays for the big stages. (5) **Seeing the next one**: tester collection reports a crash the boot script found, sends a take's start at once instead of on the 20 second timer, and the device snapshot carries the graphics mode and why. Ref: #316 (the clip), #216 (the pose loader), #221 (motion on <html>), #405.

413. 2026-11-05 · **A take the phone stopped hearing ends there, and a start that throws lets go of the mic** · From the crash investigation's audio lens, on the tester data. iOS takes the mic from a page sent to the background, and the take's clock ran on over silence until Stop, so the take uploaded with its end missing (Alan's third take on 4 Nov stops exactly at the hide; Mathias on 2 Nov). Now a take ends when its track is muted or ended, or when the page hides on an iPhone or iPad, and what was heard is scored; #406's guards turn away one too short. Hidden on a desktop or Android keeps recording, since the mic stays there. Also: a start that throws after the device opened stops the recorder, the tracks and the context instead of leaving them live behind the permission card; Score it again (and the back-online retry) does nothing while a take is starting or live, where it used to orphan the take with the mic on; the video recorder's stop has the audio recorder's three-second limit; teardown lets the camera's frames go; Start over clears the pose ring. From the WebKit lens: iOS 26.3 to 26.6 (Nick's and Vincent's 26.5.2 among them) can refuse the mic with Allow already set after a media reset until the browser is quit (bugs.webkit.org 319706), so the iPhone mic help ends with that step and a refused start reaches tester collection with the browser's error name (`record_start` with `blocked`); and Demos's clip asks Apple devices for the H.264 file first, which the iPhone decodes in hardware, instead of VP9. Ref: #406, #407, #412.

415. 2026-11-05 · **A sheet leaves the edge already moving, and it is solid while it rises** · Timothy, of the word sheet: "its so laggy, shld be smooth gotta add math, make it fast then slow." Measured first, at a 4x slower CPU: the tap reached its first frame in 27ms with no long task, so the lag was the motion, twice over. (1) The rise was SPRING.sheet from rest, and a critically damped spring from rest has no speed at t = 0: 2% of the way in the first frame, 17% at 50ms. Now it launches at SHEET_LAUNCH (0.6 w0, `lib/spring.ts`), the same spring with a starting speed: 14% at the first frame, 39% at 50ms, 64% at 100ms, the same slow landing, no overshoot, 562ms (`--spring-sheet-in`, mirrored and tested like every house spring; Overlay's mid-rise hand-off reads the launched curve). (2) The sheet is a child of the dialog, and the dialog was what faded in, so every sheet rose half see-through: at 100ms the transcript and the orange footer showed through the word sheet, and the phone redrew the sheet and the frosted page as one group each frame. The dim and the frost are now the dialog's ::before, behind the sheet, and only they fade; lite and reduced transparency follow it there. Ref: #222, #242, apple-design §4.

416. 2026-11-05 · **The bug hunt's fixes, a way to stop testing, and guests share their address's scoring room** · Timothy: "Can we logic test and bug test the app." Six finders (accounts, recording, progress logic, testers, server and security, a browser walk) and a skeptic per area: 39 findings survived, 3 were refuted. Fixed in four groups on disjoint files (fix/progress, fix/recording, fix/accounts, fix/server, merged here): a take too short to score is no longer a measurement anywhere (Today's tiles and card, the Log, Lessons, records, badges, the end note: `measured(row)` in lib/trait-readings.ts re-runs the engine's own substance gate); Pace's line names its zone instead of "inside 100"; a line at the bottom of the scale can be missed; the dial reads only the weeks before this one, so the line holds all week; Today turns over at midnight when left open; the streak roll and its line play once a day, not on every take; an unscored take ends on its streak or its count; Start over sits clear of Record, sends record_stop, and leaving mid-take lets the mic's listeners go; the think time running out records instead of starting another; Q&A from Practice goes back to Practice; an open redirect through `next=/.//host` is closed; a lapsed or unreachable session is not sent to sign up; the gate's Back, the inbox's Back and sign-out no longer loop to Create your account; a fresh device's deep link meets the gate; #414's overlay lets go when the sign-in leg is dismissed; Allow notifications says what iPhone Safari needs; Whisper faults that are ours give the slot back; Hostile spends its own allowance and counts the week in the phone's zone; Upload states the real 4.5MB limit; a tester whose app opened offline is collected once it is back; the record_stop of a take ended by hiding rides the hide beacon; Messenger is named Messenger; saved words are capped at 500 an account (0024); the coach retries stop inside the route's time budget. Timothy's two calls on what went to him: (a) "Add 'Stop testing'": collection still carries on after the test (#405), and a tester can now end it from the Testing Ethos page (two taps, the second naming what stops); the row is marked `stopped_at` (0025) rather than removed, so what they agreed to and what they sent stays, and the collect route, the questions and the phone all read the mark. The line saying testing goes on after 15 November is on that page and in Privacy; the consent itself gets it at its next version, since changing its text now would ask all twenty testers to agree again mid-test. (b) "Add a per-address ceiling": every guest session on one address shares 30 scorings an hour and 90 a day (`guestip`), on top of each guest's own 10 and 30; accounts never count against it, and a refused or failed take gives both slots back. Then an independent review of the merged fixes (three lenses, each verified) found what the fixes broke, and it is fixed here too: Hostile's new refunds had made it free (a short answer or a failed verdict gave the slot back after Whisper or the model had run), so a slot now stays once a paid call ran, and guests share their address's room there as well; a stopped tester read as a tester again on every screen but collection, so the phone now treats a stopped row as no row (Settings' plain code row, no tile or questions, the code page to start again), entering the code and agreeing restarts testing, and a stop survives signing in; 0025 was applied to production before this code, which reads its column; an unscored take no longer ends on "Recorded, measured, kept." or "Clean take."; the streak roll reads a new day when the recording screen stays open past midnight; and Settings says On after 15 November, since collection carries on there and that row is the way to Stop testing. Ref: #55, #405, #406, #409, #410, #413, #414.

Testers spent a 1.7s median on Practice and made one scored game recording in 81, because a game existed only before Record: after Stop it was a daily recording, and its ×2 XP was shown and not paid. Now: every game has three levels that change only the staging (Q&A's cut-in early, then twice; Speed rush 45, 30, then 20 seconds; Interview's question hidden until Record, then a follow-up), and stars, the Index and every threshold stay the same at every level. The server measures the round's one thing (Q&A's held silence after each cut-in, Speed rush's time to spare, Interview's last word inside 1:00 to 1:30) and stores it in `reps.round` (migration 0026, `lib/round.ts`); two counted rounds on two days open the next level, three 3-star days at level 3 master the game, and nothing goes down (`lib/ladder.ts`). Demos cuts in himself, speech-anchored: his listening pose swaps to speaking mid-word with a bubble over him, never over Stop (`lib/round-live.ts`). The score step leads with the round's own card (the strip, the dial or the window bar, then the XP line), the cut-in is marked on the pause map and Hear it back, the wait says what the phone knows ("Demos cut in at 0:17.", "The clock stopped it at 0:30."), and one sound plays on a real win only. One best per game and level stands before Record as a plaque (Timothy, 6 Nov: "it is an important milestone"), on the tile, and as "Best round yet" when broken; Tomorrow names the ladder, and a level opened or a game mastered is the closing moment. Practice's tiles carry the level, the pips and the best, and a mastered glyph turns sage. A free account gets one Premium game a day in rotation as a full round (game of the day), and after that round, on the way out and once per game, the plum sheet leads with its real number ("Interview, level 1. 1:14 inside the window." then "Play it any day."). Hostile Q&A gets a door from Practice for a free account's weekly take, says its question in order, lands the verdict a row at a time, stores it on the take, and ends on Another claim. The XP shown is the XP paid: a game pays its staged mods on any plan where the round is open, Interruption only when Demos actually cut in, times the level (×1, ×1.25, ×1.5, capped at 3), and the server recomputes it; the server also holds the game of the day itself, so a free account's Premium round is measured, stored and paid as the game only on the speaker's own day it is free and only as that day's first, and any other is kept as a plain recording with no round. The boss is the week's benchmark: 90 seconds to read, 90 to explain, no mods, Voice by default, no Ready screen, the last six weeks' first Index as a record, a spent free week opening See your take, and the delta against last week's first scored boss. Breaks, each on purpose: M07's walk with no exit before its end, for games only, since a round is on its first step and Another round sits quietly under The numbers; #181's mods on the boss lobby, since a benchmark with conditions you choose measures nothing week to week; #317's Premium wall, which now opens one free game of the day, because a free account should play every paid game within two days before it is asked to pay; the boss's 4-minute read, which became 90 seconds so the read and the talk are one sitting. Smaller calls the builders made: the stars drop to 32px on a game's score step so Demos keeps 120 or more; the cut-in's line sets at the introduction's lead size (F1), breaking DESIGN 9's 17 because it is read in the corner of the eye mid-sentence, in one tailed SpeechBubble wherever he says it (over him in the column, beside his 36px head under 600pt tall and in Video); the round card's XP line is the first XP on any results step; Hostile is a full-width row door on a Premium account's grid (it has no ladder); the free-today and Hostile tiles stand under the Premium head above the card; Hostile's question screen stands Demos on the claim card; Interview's level 2 and 3 lines no longer repeat the card's "Press Record to see the question."; See your take comes back to the boss. The envelope moves no star, no Index and no threshold (the pause score never reads it, which the open queue holds for Timothy): it moves only the pause list's verdicts and their headline, where a take queued from before the review, sampled about 17 times a second under a label of 20, is read at its real rate (on the regression review's 72s and 89s test takes, 12 and 15 pauses "with a sound in it", 9 and 11 of them wrong, become the true 3 and 4), while the phone's own envelope, marked as sampled on the take's clock, is never stretched and leaves a span it had no frames for unknown (#122). Migration 0026 is NOT applied to production yet and must be applied before this ships: until then a round is still scored and paid, but no round is kept, so no level opens, no best is held, and Hostile's verdict is shown and not stored. Shots are in `docs/look/games/`. Ref: #36, #37 (difficulty buys XP, never stars), #53 (the best compares against a snapshot from before the take), #140 (the chime on a real win only), #157, #194 (a game's staged mods paid on any plan), #198 (the ask at a moment, never a deny wall), M07, #181, #317; the plan's research by mechanic name: the speech-anchored interruption, the level ladder with a two-day rule, the personal best per level, the free daily rotation of paid content, and the ask after the taste.

The mic heard it, the transcript didn't.", "2 of your pauses had a sound in them"), then `tier1Scores` judged the same pauses again from the words alone, so a gap the pause list called filled scored as a landing: Pause 73 beside that headline (regression review, engine P1). Now the pauses are judged once and `tier1Scores` scores those verdicts (`reportJudged`, lib/pause-quality.ts), so the Pause score, the Index, the pause list and its headline agree by construction (#121). A sound has to be heard to cost a pause: a take with no envelope, a gap the phone never heard (null since #420), and every envelope without the take's clock mark (sent by a phone still on main's recorder, or an outbox replay of a take recorded on one) keep the verdict their words give. That last one also changes what such a take shows. Main's sampler runs at or under its label of 20 and takes nothing while the page is hidden, and the server cannot tell how far off it is: refitted (more than 5% short), its samples are spread evenly over the take, right on a page that never hid and seconds off after one that did (the regression lane's hidden-page replays read 8 or 9 filled for 2 real); read as sent (up to 5% short, which `fitEnvelopeRate` leaves alone: main's sampler runs about 19.1 a second on a 240Hz screen, or at 20 with a short hidden stretch), a late sample lands up to 3s early and a pause is judged on the next sentence's speech (a 58s take of nine clean held pauses read 6 to 9 filled from 19.0 to 19.7 a second, Pause 0 and Index 572 where the words give 91 and 709). So only an envelope marked as sampled on the take's clock judges a pause (`pauseEnvelope`, lib/envelope.ts, amended 6 Nov from "any envelope not refitted"), and only a game round still reads an unmarked one, through `fitEnvelopeRate`. Nothing stored moves: the route only inserts and answers a resend with the row it already has, and every place that shows a past recording's Index, stars, dimensions or traits (the Log, Today's tiles and week, You, the traits and their levels, insights, records and rewards, the schedule, the share card and Day 1 vs now, the boss lobby, the tester views) reads its stored columns; `lib/stored-scores.test.ts` holds a row stored before this fix to its numbers on those paths. 426. 2026-11-07 · **Sign-up with an email or Google that already has an account signs in, where it stands** · Timothy: "If i try sign up instead of sign in just sign me in instead of sending me back". Every way to sign up ends on two doors: the email form on /signup (reached from the introduction's "Use an email instead", the account gate, the save-progress prompt's "Save my progress", Today's "Keep them", You's and Settings' "Create my account", the paywall's account ask) and Continue with Google on /signup and the introduction's account ask. Before: a guest's email form (email only, #142) hit `updateUser`, which answers 422 `email_exists` for an address with an account, and the form said "That email already has an Ethos account. Sign in instead." so the person went to /signin and typed it all again; a device with no session ran `signUp`, which with confirmations on answers 200 with a made-up user (`identities: []`, no session) and sends nothing, so the form showed "Check your inbox" for a mail that never comes; Google was switched to a sign-in by #414, but its welcome said "Everything you've already recorded came with you." (the guest's recordings stay on the guest) and the screen left when the switch had already run was "That Google account is taken" with a Sign in that went to /signin. Now (`createAccount`, `signInExisting`, lib/auth.ts, read from the response's error code and words and `user.identities`, never guessed): **with a password typed**, sign-up meeting a confirmed account (the empty-identities user, or `user_already_exists` with confirmations off) signs in with the same email and password at once and lands where a sign-in lands (the same `next=`, the account gate released by the account's session, testing carried exactly as /signin carries it: both now share `passwordSignIn`); no line on the way, since the page it lands on is theirs and a line would only flash on a page that is leaving. **A guest's form** keeps the email and asks for that account's password in place: the title becomes sign-in's "Welcome back", "That email has an Ethos account. Enter its password to sign in." in FormError's slot in the ink (`FormNote`), the password field with the cursor in it and Forgot? on its row, Sign in the tap, "Already have one?" gone; typing another address makes it a sign-up again. **A wrong password** stays there: "That email has an Ethos account. This password doesn't match it.", the email kept, the password emptied and focused, Sign in and Forgot? (which opens /auth/forgot with the address filled, `withEmail`/`emailFrom`). **An account that never confirmed**: a sign-up with no session gets that account's link again from GoTrue and its real user, made more than a minute before the link it just sent; a guest's sign-in hears `email_not_confirmed` and the link goes again (`resend`, type signup, to the callback with `next`, marked `resent=1` since the review below); a link sent under a minute ago ("only request this after") counts as sent. Each ends on the inbox: "x already has an account that was never confirmed. We've sent its link again from hello@speakethos.com. Open it, then sign in.", its Sign in opening /signin with the email filled, never the form again. Nothing makes a second account (one `updateUser` or one `signUp`, never both, never twice) and nothing signs in to an account but the one the typed email names. **Google**: every sign-up door is /signup's button or the introduction's, both `signInWithGoogle("signup")`; with no session that is a plain Google sign-in, which already lands in an existing account, and with a guest it links and meets #414's switch, which now signs in as a sign-in begun where Google was tapped (`signInWithGoogle`'s `from`), so the callback says "Signed in as x." and never that the recordings came along, and the page to go on to comes from that attempt when Supabase drops it. The screen left after a switch that already ran or was backed out of is "That Google account already has Ethos", "Sign in with it.", Google's own button signing in in one tap (D49: no terracotta on it) and "Use an email instead" with `next`. **The guest's own data**: since #410 a guest makes the account before recording, so a guest who meets this has nothing recorded. A pre-#410 guest's recordings stay on the guest when it signs in, as every sign-in leaves them (#414); that is what /signin already does, and keeping them would mean moving recordings, audio, XP and streak days between users, which this does not do. So it is said before the tap: the tile reads "Signing in to that account leaves them on this device. Use another email to keep them.", and the Google caption promising they attach is hidden while the form signs in. Testing rides along (`carried_from`, #416), unsent recordings stay the guest's (the outbox only sends its own owner's), and an introduction not yet synced goes to the account on its next open, as on every sign-in. Tests: lib/auth-existing.test.ts drives each response shape (email_exists, the empty-identities user, user_already_exists, the wrong password, email_not_confirmed with its resend, a link too soon, a new email, the switch's attempt, the carried address). Gates on a production build: scripts/check-signup-existing.mjs 66/66 (new: the guest and the no-session walks to signed in on `next`, the carry, the wrong password and Forgot?, the unconfirmed inbox both ways, another email, a pre-#410 guest's tile, Google from /signup, the introduction and the account gate to signed in with no sign-up screen on the way, the leftover screen in one tap), check-google-return 45/45, check-onboarding 83/83, check-apple 28/28. Shots at 390x844 light and dark and 375x667, before and after: docs/look/signup-existing/. Review, 7 Nov: three findings, fixed as written here. (1) A crafted link could hand a guest's account to someone else, new in this change: the form filled its email from `?email=` on sign-up as well as sign-in, a guest's sign-up shows only that field, and its one tap is `updateUser({email})` on the guest, whose confirmation link (to /auth/reset?first=1) gives whoever opens it the guest's user, its first password, and every recording and transcript after. Nothing in the app links a sign-up to an address, so only sign-in reads it now (`carriedEmail`); /auth/forgot keeps its fill, since its mail goes to the address's owner. (2) The link an unconfirmed account got again said "Everything you've already recorded came with you." when opened here (the guest's recordings stay on the guest) and lost the testing the guest had joined, since a mail app opens it in a new tab where Google's sessionStorage stash is not: the guest's carry token now waits in localStorage for the link's day (`keepCarryForLink`, dropped on sign-out), the link lands on `/auth/callback?resent=1`, and the callback carries it once (`carryKeptForLink`) and says only "x is confirmed." (`callbackWelcome`). The token is the guest's access token, so a link opened after it lapses carries nothing, as the server refuses it. (3) Google from /signup by a guest holding recordings, whose Google account already has Ethos, was switched to a sign-in with nothing said, though the email path now warns before its sign-in (not a regression, #414): the attempt now remembers what the guest holds (`OAuthAttempt.recordings`), the switch waits for it (`warnsFirst`), and the callback's taken screen says "Signing in to it leaves your 22 recordings on this device." over Google's own button, with "Keep them with an email" back to the form; a return Supabase lands anywhere else is sent to that screen. A guest holding nothing is switched at once, as before. The tile and the caption under Google stay: they describe the account the form makes, and where it cannot make one, both paths now say what stays before signing in. Three principles from the research decided it: progressive disclosure in two levels, the few things everyone must take in on the path and the rest one labelled tap away, pulled and never pushed (NN/g; Chiviacowsky, Wulf and Lewthwaite 2012: learners who choose when to see feedback learn more), which is his "compulsory" made voluntary; glanceability and BLUF (Gouveia's 5-second glance, NN/g's 20% of words read: the answer first, in one look); and redundancy (Sweller, Mayer's coherence: say each thing once). Now a take ends on one screen with no scroll (held at 390x844 and 375x667 for every variant shot, and at 320x568 for a lesson practice and a tester): the header's back (the practice's name, at BackLink's geometry, named "Back to the lesson", "Back to Today" or "Back to Practice"), a lesson's SegmentBar, the stars, the Index and a bare "▲ 3" (its words for a screen reader), one earned line only when something was earned (`earnedLine`: a ladder moment, "Best yet, past 690", the trait's record, "Best round yet"; a Premium video take's Presence and a boss's Accuracy in ink), Demos saying one measured line about the practice's own trait with one moment that plays and one target for next time ("4 fillers, first at ▶0:02." / "Practice 2: under 4.", "3 landed, 1 searching at ▶0:20." / "Next time: land 4."; a game's ladder's first sentence, "1 of 2 counted."; an unscored take's reason, `lib/glance.ts`), the row door "The whole recording", and the tap with Retake under it, live from the first frame.
