428. 2026-11-07 · **The word sheet answers for the sentence, or says keep** · Timothy, with two screenshots of "Try instead": "the try instead words are shittt ... look look". "things" in "I think today's the day I switch things up." offered points, ideas, parts, details, steps; "dollar" in "You know, I love to contribute to the billion dollar corporations that pay one dollar per hour for child labor." offered buck, cent, penny, pound, euro, quarter. Diagnosis (reproduced with the repo's own functions, and on the live build in `docs/look/try-instead/*-before-*`): the first came from the curated map on the phone, instantly, with the sentence never read (`CURATED.things`), and the map's list is the word's other meanings, none of which fits inside an idiom; the second came from Haiku, whose v1 prompt asked a "thesaurus" for three to six words that fit the grammar, so a word that was already right had no honest answer and category siblings passed. One root under both: each path answered "what words are like this word?", and the question is "what would this speaker have done better to say, here?". The rule: an option passes when, swapped into the sentence in place of what it replaces, it keeps the meaning and its strength and says it more precisely, more vividly or in fewer words, the way people talk; when nothing passes the answer is keep, and keep is a full answer. Now: the curated map is deleted and every tap asks the server with its sentence and the word's place (`at`, so the two "dollar"s are two questions); one Haiku call reads the word marked in its sentence and returns swap (`replaces`, the word or the idiom or intensifier phrase it sits in, and one to three options) or keep (one line from a fixed enum, `KEEP_NOTES`, so every line on the sheet is Ethos's own copy); `cleanAnswer` (lib/words.ts) holds the shape, drops the replaced words, dashes, vague words and fillers the phrase did not hold and the banned register, and when `replaces` is not whole words of the sentence over the tapped word keeps a keep for the word and fails a swap (502, the sheet's retry); both caches are v2 (`ethos.words.v2` on the phone with the v1 store deleted, a versioned key with the place in it on the server), and an old-version reply is a failure, never a list. The sheet: a skeleton head over three rows while it reads; swap is "Try instead" with "In place of “switch things up”" and the sentence's set-apart span widening to the phrase when it replaces more than the word, Save keeping the phrase as the word said (within saved_words' 40 characters); keep is "Keep it." over its line, with no rows and no door; the region glides between heights on the base spring. Eval (stand-ins, the 40 cases in the scratch eval, lists corrected so failing words count as failures): the map alone 2/40 with 68 bad words; the v2 draft prompt Haiku 37/40, Sonnet 35/40, every verdict, span and keep note right in both, three failures shared by both (the prompt's), so the FINAL prompt fixes five patterns by rule and states none of the cases' answers. Model: Claude Haiku 5.5 at `low` effort, thinking adaptive, system prompt cached, no fallbacks (Haiku has none; a refusal is a 502, never remembered): Sonnet was not better (35 against 37, every Haiku failure also its), and someone is watching a skeleton. The live gate (38/40 on the corrected lists, 0 bad options on Timothy's two and the injection case) still needs a key and Timothy's yes; if it fails, fix the shared pattern, then Haiku `medium`, then Sonnet 5.5 `low` with fallbacks. Calls made here, against the task where they differ: one to three options, not two to five (the FINAL prompt's own examples give one, and the eval found Sonnet's fourth options held its worst words); no labelled curated fallback offline, since the design decided the map goes and a caption over "points, ideas, parts" is the same list Timothy objected to: offline says "You're offline, so the alternatives didn't load." with Try again; "Already precise." became "It's already the precise word." (COPY-RULES: no fragments); a token that is only digits is kept as "A number says it exactly." without a call; set phrases ("good luck", "the very end", `SET_PHRASES`) lose the vague underline so the paragraph and the sheet's keep agree; Your words' empty example is "very cold instead: freezing". Camera: `scripts/look-try-instead.mjs` (before against the v1 build with LOOK_API=v1, after at 390x844 both themes and 375x667, strips of loading to keep and loading to phrase). Ref: #411, #415, D15, DESIGN.md (nothing teleports). Review (same day), two fixes: a swap whose `replaces` could not be found used to fall back to the word with its one-word options, so "plans", written for a phrase the sentence does not hold, would read "I switch plans up", the grammar-only fit this entry exists to stop; it is now a failed answer, as the design's §2.3 had it. And the service worker's cache goes to `ethos-v17`: pages are cached network-first and scripts cache-first, so a page last opened before this change would run, offline, the scripts that still hold the curated map; activate now deletes that cache (and the worker's comments lose "rep" and their dashes).

429. 2026-11-08 · **Say this instead keeps your meaning, or says nothing** · Timothy, 8 Nov, "sure", to: give Demos's word upgrade ("Say this instead" in the results sheet) the word sheet's rule from #428, and clean the banned register out of every prompt sent to a model. Diagnosis: the coach was asked for "ONE word/phrase upgrade ... a stronger swap", with nothing about the sentence or the meaning and no way to say none, so the pair in every camera shot, "the thing about" to "what matters about", turned a framing phrase into a claim of importance; the only check was a raw substring (it passed "art" inside "start" and phrases across a full stop, failed "really really good" against Whisper's "really, really good", and a failure retried the whole Opus call and could cost the take its Index), and the note was free text in the "X, not Y" shape COPY-RULES rations. The rule: the upgrade is the speaker's own words from one sentence (1 to 8), and what replaces them (1 to 6) keeps what they meant at the same strength, adds no fact, and is more precise, more vivid or shorter, in their register; otherwise the answer is none, and none is a full answer. Now: the coach's reply says `verdict` upgrade or none with a `why` enum, the prompt's Task A item 3 points at a new last section (`COACH_SYSTEM`, exported and cached; Task B through the pause vocabulary byte-identical, model and effort unchanged, so the Index the testers rate through 15 Nov does not move); `lib/upgrade.ts` `cleanUpgrade` drops, with a logged reason and never the speaker's words, an upgrade not said verbatim inside one sentence (case, punctuation and apostrophes aside, whole words), only fillers, with a dash or brackets, the same words, the banned register, hype, a vague word, a filler, an extra intensifier, a written word, or a number or name the take did not have; a dropped upgrade is never a retry and the take keeps its coach and Index; the note is one of three fixed lines (`UPGRADE_NOTES`); no upgrade means Demos's read is Kept alone and no lexicon row (`lib/db.ts` guarded, it threw after the insert before); the Log builds its coach without a supply (it dropped Kept and the judged dimensions) and hides a stored pair that fails the checks any pair must (`shownSupply`, so old "um" to "(pause)" rows stop showing). Eval (stand-ins on the 24 cases, `lib/__fixtures__/upgrade-cases.json`, the corrected lists): the draft prompt Haiku 24/24 and Sonnet 24/24, 0 bad upgrades shown, every none on precise, injection, fillers and stance right; 24/24 is an upper bound (the cases are short and the runs agree with the lists' first entries), so the FINAL prompt removes three hints and two contradictions by rule and states no case's answer (leak check 1 of 24, Task A's own unchanged "went well"); the structural check alone keeps every good pair and drops 22 of the 139 concrete bad pairs (vague 10, hype 6, bookish 3, number 3), the rest is the prompt's job. The live gate (22/24 on `claude-opus-5` at low, 0 bad shown, plus the Tier-2 drift check against the old prompt) still needs a key and Timothy's yes. The banned register: the one word in any string sent to a model was the coach's "The gap is theirs; the reps are ours.", now "They already know the gap; the numbers measure it."; comments in `lib/coach.ts`, `lib/accuracy.ts` and `lib/transcribe.ts` say recording or take, and test fixtures lost "1 filler this rep", "Clean rep!" and "train speech under pressure". `lib/register.ts` is one pattern for the register (the word sheet's options, the upgrade, Demos's copy), and `lib/model-prompts.test.ts` drives every model caller with the SDK and fetch mocked through every turn it can send (first ask, schema retry, rules retry; every cold topic and Hostile claim) and fails on the register in anything sent, or a prose dash in what Ethos writes to a model that writes for a person; a coverage check fails a new caller until it is driven. Calls made here, against the design where they differ: the reply's supply is read loosely and handed to `cleanUpgrade`, so even a malformed one is a drop, not a schema retry; the register check on Demos's own copy (hard, as the design recommended) ignores quoted spans and any word the speaker said, so a speaker who uses one of those words about their own week is not a violation; "(pause)" is dropped as a bracket, not stripped to "pause"; every fixture lexicon row became an honest pair, not only the one; `lib/rep-result-all.test.ts` keeps its fixture so its snapshot stays the e2fd1cd guard, and the snapshot changed only by the arrow's new wrapper. Look loop (`scripts/look-say-instead.mjs`, `docs/look/say-instead/`): the old pair, an honest one and none, 390x844 both themes, and the Log with none; the honest pair's longer from wrapped with the arrow stranded at the end of its line, so the arrow now travels with the upgrade ("→ an impossible week" on the second line), in the sheet and on the Log. Ref: #428, #427, #12, #19, CLAUDE.md (the one banned thing), COPY-RULES. Review (same day): the prompt is `prompt-final.txt` byte for byte, and against 3441d40 only the banned line, Task A item 3 and the appended section changed; three ways `cleanUpgrade` dropped an honest upgrade, one way it changed one, and one way the Log hid an old row, now fixed in `lib/upgrade.ts` with tests: "I'd", "I'll", "I’ve" past the first word read as a name the speaker never said ("what I'd do is" was `new-name`); the conjunction "so" counted as an intensifier ("and because of that we" to "so we", the long way round the prompt asks for, was `intensifier`), and now "so" counts only where it modifies the next word; a closing apostrophe was stripped as a quote ("my parents'" showed as "my parents"), and single quotes now go only when they wrap the whole string; the modifier-letter apostrophe (U+02BC) did not match "didn't" (`not-said`); and `shownSupply` held old rows to today's length limits (6 words, 40 characters), hiding honest pairs stored under the old 120, when the design's read-side guard is the checks any pair must (§4.3, checks 3 to 8). The retry turn that names a register word the coach wrote (`copy uses "<word>"`) echoes the model's own word, as a quote of the speaker would; no prompt Ethos writes says one. Superseded in part by #507: the lexicon section and its test on You became Say it better.

430. 2026-11-09 · **Reminders reach people: armed in the tap, asked on the glance, sent every hour** · Timothy, 8 to 9 Nov: "a lot of people have been slacking the last couple of days. Its a sign that real users wont stick ethier", then "yes" to reminders that actually reach people. Measured on production (aggregates only, 9 Nov): 21 active testers; 7 recorded on 8 Nov, 6 recorded 1 to 3 days then stopped, 6 never recorded; on days without a take 13 people opened Today for a median 6.7 s and left; 14 of 19 run Ethos from the Home Screen (iOS 18.7, where web push works), and only 4 had a row in `push_subscriptions`. Of the reminder taps, "Evening 6 pm" on the results (2) both had rows, "Night, 9 pm" in the introduction (2) neither, "Lunch 12 pm" on the results (1) none. Three failures, each enough alone (the investigation: `reminders.md`, replayed through `lib/push.ts`): (1) an hour picked in the introduction is only a pref (#250 asks nothing there, rightly) and `reminderDue` went quiet once any hour was set, so it was never armed, on that phone or a new one; (2) the results ask came only behind a leaving exit and the walls, and people glance and swipe away; (3) GitHub ran the hourly `17 * * * *` about 4 times a day (60 runs 25 Oct to 9 Nov, gaps to 10 h 40 m, every run a 200), so a chosen hour with no tick in its three-hour window was never served: 28 of 60 hour-days, none on 6 Nov, and the 6 pm subscribers got nothing on Sunday 8 Nov. The run logs show 7 pushes in ten days. Built: **the arm** (`lib/reminders.ts` `armInTap`) makes `pushManager.subscribe` the tap's first await on a registration fetched at mount, so an installed iPhone's Allow prompt comes from the tap (no reg yet: the permission is asked in the tap, and a missing worker fails after 6 s instead of hanging); it says on only after POST /api/push answers 200, then keeps the hour, the acknowledgement and the account's hour (`onboarding.reminder_hour`, an update, never an insert). Every way on goes through it: **the glance** asks on the day's first scored take whenever this phone has no acknowledged reminder (`lib/reminder-ask.ts` `reminderAsk`; the one expectation in `reminder-ask.test.ts` that flips, "never once an hour is set", is the point), in the cue's slot inside Demos's bubble, grey, never terracotta: "Same time tomorrow?" over "Remind me at 3 pm" (the hour the take started, rounded down, inside quiet hours 9 pm or 7 am; the hour already chosen first, as "You picked 9 pm.", which is how the introduction's pick is armed at the first moment with an account and a gesture) with the hour as the phone's own picker split off the pill, and Not now (twice rests it a week); then "Tomorrow, 3 pm." or "That didn't save. Try again"; Don't Allow says "Notifications are off for Ethos. Turn them on in Settings, Notifications, Ethos." once and it never asks again; an iPhone's Safari tab, which has no notification API, gets "Add Ethos to your Home Screen to get reminders. Tap Share, then Add to Home Screen." once, never a tap that cannot work, and the take's hour goes on the account for the installed app; Off in Settings or the introduction is an answer it respects. Its words ride on the glance's ceiling up to 16 (`GLANCE_WORDS_ASK`, a named exception like the tester's: once a day at most) and the cue gives up its slot; under 700pt the question drops and the tap stays, and beside a tester's question the pill becomes a line of its own words (the pill wrapped "Remind me at 3 / pm"); no scroll at 390x844, 375x667 and 320x568, tester included. The exit's reminder beat is gone (`exitBeat`). **Settings**: the switch itself asks, in its tap, through the same arm; "Saving" until the server answers; a failure on a Home Screen phone says "The reminder didn't reach the server. Try again", never "install it" to someone who has; the blocked line names iPhone Settings on an iPhone; the Safari tab gets the two steps; `new Notification` before the save is gone; "Send a test" (`POST /api/push/test`, own endpoint, once a minute, never touching `last_sent_on`) proves the whole path in seconds. **The sender**: from the chosen hour until quiet hours begin, the same day (43 of 60 on the same real ticks; every hour 7 to 21 on 8 Nov; on 7 Nov 19 to 21 had no tick between 18:07 and 22:00, which no window fixes, only an hourly clock), a late send titled "Today's practice" rather than an hour that has passed (the investigation's objection to widening, answered in the words); the day claimed on the row before sending (one conditional update), so the database's tick and GitHub's in the same minute ring once, and handed back when the push service fails; `PUSH_TICK_SECRET` accepted beside `CRON_SECRET` in constant time; the answer says `subs, due, done, unsure, taken, sent, failed, dropped`. The expectation "21:00 is past the window" in `lib/push.test.ts` changed with it (the point). **The clock**: `supabase/migrations/0027_push_tick.sql`, written and not applied: pg_cron at minute 1 of every hour through pg_net, URL and secret read from Vault only, a no-op without them; replayed, every hour 7 to 21 served at HH:01 every day, once, with GitHub still ticking (`lib/push-ticks.test.ts`). GitHub stays the backup at :07, :29 and :48 with a concurrency group. **Words** (`lib/reminder-copy.ts`): "Your 6 pm practice" / "Last time: 4 fillers in 62 seconds." (the newest take of 30 s or more), "It takes about a minute." before a first take; no streak to keep, no "day two" line that traced to nothing, no chained fragments; the expectation "names the streak" in `lib/reminders.test.ts` changed with it (the point); sw.js's fallback says the same. A tester's phone files each outcome as a tap with role `reminder` ("Reminder: failed, server 502"), no new event name and no migration. Production steps, and who: `docs/reminders-ops.md` (Timothy: the secret, Vercel, the extensions, Vault, the migration, the check queries, his iPhone). The boss-week test in `lib/analyze-route.test.ts` now pins its clock: it read today's week once 9 Nov passed `recordedAt`'s slack, failing on c134ec8. Shots before and after, 390x844 light and dark, the short phones in light: `docs/look/comeback-reminders/` (`scripts/look-reminders.mjs`; `scripts/look-glance.mjs` now shoots the ask on the glance). **The review (9 Nov), fifteen findings, each reproduced before it was fixed.** (1) **The clock is the ship gate, not a follow-up:** replayed on GitHub's real ticks, the code alone served 8 pm on 4 of 10 days in Melbourne and 9 pm, both introduction pickers' hour, on 2, and no window can serve a 9 pm pick that quiet hours end at 22; STATE's Open list carries the hourly clock as owed and blocking (`docs/reminders-ops.md` steps 2 to 7, or the two-minute cron-job.org route). So a dead clock is seen, the sender notes every tick it lets in (`push-tick:<secret>:<caller>` rows in `rate_limits`, 0006, already on production, so the record works the day it merges without another migration; two days kept; `lib/push-clock.ts`) and /active draws a Reminder clock: each caller's last tick, how many of the last 24 hours it reached, and "No tick in the last hour. Reminders are not going out on time." when none came (fixture shots `docs/look/comeback-active/`, the stopped clock included). The callers name themselves: 0027's job sends "pg_net push-tick" (amended, still not applied), push-cron.yml "github-actions push-cron". (2) **Done-today on the days the clocks change:** midnight was taken at the tick's offset, so on 5 Apr (Melbourne) and 1 Nov (New York) a 00:30 take was missed and the reminder rang after practice, and on 4 Oct last night's 23:30 counted as today; `localDayStartIn` takes the offset in force at that midnight (13:00Z, 04:00Z, 14:00Z), tested through the route with a reps stand-in that honours its gte. (3) **TTL:** every push carried web-push's four weeks, so a phone that was off got it at 2 am or the next day; now until the earlier of quiet hours and midnight (a minute at least), urgency normal, topic `ethos-daily`; the test push 60 s under its own topic. (4) **Tomorrow means tomorrow:** a 12 pm picked at 3:30 pm rang at 4:01 pm while Settings said tomorrow; POST /api/push reads the row first and stamps today on a new or changed hour already past (the re-arm on every open, same hour, leaves the stamp). (5) The Safari tab's install line wrote the take's hour to the account, and the installed app read it as "You picked 4 pm." and as on: it saves nothing now (`saveAccountHour` lost `onlyIfUnset`). (6) `ADMIN_EMAILS` is in the ops doc's Vercel step. (7) **A secret under 32 characters is ignored**, logged once (`TICK_SECRET_MIN`, `lib/cron-auth.ts`); `PUSH_TICK_SECRET` is in `.env.example` with the openssl line. The floor holds for `CRON_SECRET` too, whose length nobody here can read: a short one now fails GitHub's run with 401, and the owed step says to check it. (8) **The ask stopped coming back daily:** a showing left with no tap counts as a Not now, settled at the next glance (`settleUnanswered`), because swiping the app away ends no React tree and an unmount hook never runs in exactly the case it is for; two declined showings rest it 7 days and each one after that 30, never daily again; the cue keeps its slot on every day the ask does not show. (11) **One question at a time:** on a tester's glance the ask is held, unspent, until "Did 665 feel right?" saves, then takes the cue's slot (it stacked 11 taps and shrank Demos to 85px); once answered, the save line holds a line's height rather than a button's under 700pt, because 375x667 ran 21px over in that state, which no camera had shot, and under 600pt, where Demos steps off, "Answer saved" is said to a screen reader only, the ask's arrival being the sign it saved (320x568 ran 26 over; saving and a failed save show as ever). `look-reminders.mjs` now shoots the tester's glance held and answered, no scroll at 390x844, 375x667 and 320x568. (12) The compact Not now is the cue's weight, on the bubble's text edge. (14) "You picked 9 pm." is "Your reminder isn't on yet."; the pill already names the hour. (15) "To get reminders, tap Share, then Add to Home Screen." (10 words, one mention), and no ask inside another app's browser (`inAppBrowser`), which has no such steps. Expectations changed where the change is the point, each saying so: the install and chosen lines and "starts the count again" (now 30 days) in `reminder-ask.test.ts`; the secrets' values in `cron-auth`, `push-cron` and `push-cron-url` tests (32 characters or more); the active route's table list (rate_limits added). Ref: #143, #186, #427, #431, `docs/reminders-ops.md`.

The difficulty glyph stays on an open day for anyone who turned one up in the last seven days, the investigation's condition read from their own history. Breaks with the investigation: the cost sits under the lead, not under the topic, because under the topic it made the card 70px taller and put "Another topic" under the nav at 375x667; the card holds 416px on an open day and the roulette keeps that height with its way back in the link's slot, because the strip showed everything under the card jump 40px when it opened (docs/look/strips/comeback-another-topic-light.png). Tester first screen at 390x844: 44 words and 5 taps to 39 and 3; Start still 2 taps from the first second of recording. (5) **Welcome back:** a last recording two or more days ago greets "Welcome back", with the name where it fits; the greeting's measure is 52% of the column beside the rising object, so "Welcome back, Timothy" never holds one line from 360 up, and the short form keeps the welcome rather than the name (#393's fit). No count of days, no word about the streak; the flame keeps its honest 0. A local hint of the last day spoken (`lib/spoke-hint.ts`, written by Today's read and after a take) lets the card and greeting be the day's own from the first frame. (6) **Who is active:** `/active`, off the tab bar, for the founder alone: `GET /api/active` answers a confirmed email on `ADMIN_EMAILS` (new; unset is nobody) and 404s anyone else before any read; with the service role it reads testers, reps' owner and time, tester_events opens (device opens and returns), push rows' hour and zone, and freeze days, never a transcript, file, score, name or address. Rows are "Tester N" by join date counting stopped testers, so numbers never move, with the tester number, because public.testers holds no name; the founder's row is "You", last, out of the totals. Days are each tester's own calendar (tester_admin.zones), the columns Melbourne's. 432. 2026-11-09 · **The clock's secret and the founder list live in the database** · Timothy, 9 Nov: "tim.mota is me. push to main", the go for #430 and #431 with their production steps. Vercel's API refused production variables from here (403 on create and on list), so `PUSH_TICK_SECRET` and `ADMIN_EMAILS`, the two settings those steps needed, could not be set. Rather than wait on a hand step, the database holds both (`supabase/migrations/0028_db_held_secrets.sql`): the clock's secret is made inside it (`encode(extensions.gen_random_bytes(32), 'hex')` into Vault, never printed or copied), and the sender asks `public.push_tick_ok(token)` whether a bearer is it, digests compared in the database, a bearer under 32 characters never asked about, any error a refusal (`dbTickMatches`, `lib/cron-auth.ts`); `/active` lets in a confirmed, non-anonymous email in `private.founders` beside `ADMIN_EMAILS` (`public.is_founder_email`, never asked for an anonymous or unconfirmed account). Both functions are security definer with an empty search path, executable by the service role only; `private` is closed to the API. A match on the server's own secrets never reaches the database. Breaks #430's review (7) on one point: `CRON_SECRET` takes any value again, as it shipped before #430, because GitHub's backup holds it and nobody here can read its length, so the floor would have stopped the backup on deploy with nothing to show for it; `PUSH_TICK_SECRET`, new, keeps the 32. The ops doc's steps are now the database's alone (`docs/reminders-ops.md`), with how to rotate the secret. Tests: `cron-auth`, `push-cron` (the database path, its refusal, a database that cannot answer, GitHub's secret never sent to it), `active-route` (a founder from the database, anonymous and unconfirmed never asked, a stranger out), `migrations` (no secret, URL or email in 0028; service-role grants; the list closed). Ref: #430, #431, #186.

433. 2026-11-11 · **Twenty details, checked; tabs open on what the visit last read** · Timothy, 11 Nov, with a reel: "claude please do this". The reel is Aidan Murphy's (murphmaxxing) "20 small UX details from Apple, Linear, Gmail, Stripe and Amazon that make an app feel expensive", and its guide (murphmaxxing.com/vault/20-ux-details-best-apps) asks for each to be checked and the missing ones built, one at a time, with the project's checks after each. The count for Ethos: nine were already here (optimistic writes on saved words and the worn pose; the router's own prefetch of every link in view; the card's move from Today into the recorder as a view transition; sheets a finger can throw; springs that hand their speed on; safe areas; the one-time-code field where a code is typed; tabular digits; balanced heads), eight were built or completed (this entry to #439), three do not apply (a blurred placeholder has nothing to stand in for: no photo is uploaded and the art is cut-outs on clear ground; predictive back is Android's own; no menu opens on hover, so no safe triangle). The first built: **stale-while-revalidate**. Every tab opened on its skeleton and read the same rows again, so moving along the tab bar was five waits for five things the phone already had. A read that lands is now held in memory (`lib/held.ts`) and the five tabs and the lesson page draw from it in their first frame, then read again behind it: on every open, when the app is looked at again after 30 seconds, and when the network comes back (`lib/use-revalidate.ts`). Three rules keep it honest. Held is one account's: dropped whole on any change of session. A write that changes the answer drops it: a stored recording drops everything counted from recordings (`forgetRecordings`, in the outbox's send), coins spent drop the balance, a changed name, pose or plan drops the profile; a read in flight across a drop is not kept. Memory only: nothing new is written to the phone, so a transcript is where Privacy says it is and a cold open reads as before. Never held: the plan's own row and the Shop's balance (the guide's own exception: a number money rides on is read, not remembered). Never drawn while the server's page hydrates (`lib/use-warm.ts`, so the two always agree), and content drawn from it does not replay its arrival (`useStill`). A read that fails behind a drawn screen leaves the screen; only a screen with nothing to show says the read failed. Today's day turn still blanks and reads, and the reminder is armed once a visit as before. `scripts/check-held.mjs` walks the bar twice against a backend held back 350ms: 38/38; `docs/look/ux20/tabs-80ms-before-after.png` is each tab 80ms after the tap. Ref: #147, #353, #431.

434. 2026-11-11 · **A skeleton waits 300ms before it draws** · The guide's first detail: a placeholder only when the wait is long enough to need one. A bone holds its height from the first frame, as it always did (principle 8: nothing moves), and draws itself only once the read has taken 300ms (`--skeleton-wait`), in a 120ms fade; a read that lands inside that never flashes a placeholder. Reduced motion keeps the wait and drops the sweep (a wait is not motion). With #433 a skeleton is now what a first read or a slow one looks like, not what every tab switch looks like. The two cameras that photograph a loading state wait it out. `lib/motion.test.ts` holds the token and both rules. Ref: #147.

435. 2026-11-11 · **A removed word offers Undo for six seconds** · Your words took a swap off the list on one tap with no way back (the guide's Gmail detail: act at once, offer the undo, never ask first). The removal still acts at once; a toast over the tab bar says what left and offers Undo (`components/ui/Toast.tsx`, `role="status"`, ink where the page is ground, level 3, 10px over the capsule). Undo puts the same row back, its id and its date with it, so it returns to its own place (`restoreSavedWord`), and waits for the removal's own answer so it never overtakes it. Chosen over a delayed delete: a phone closed inside the window would have left the word saved. The offer lapses by itself, holds while a pointer or the keyboard is on it, and a second removal replaces the first's. Ctrl+Z (⌘Z on a Mac) undoes from anywhere that is not a field; the key is printed only where there is a keyboard to hold. The keyboard stays in the list after a removal (the next row's button). A removal or an undo that fails says so with its retry ("freezing didn't come back."). `scripts/check-undo.mjs`: 22/22. Ref: #411.

436. 2026-11-11 · **Uploads and Hostile takes travel under a key** · The recording screen keys every take in its outbox and the server stores a key once (0016). Upload and Hostile's banked take posted with no key, so a send whose answer was lost, sent again, filed the same recording twice: two rows, two days' XP. Both now carry a key made when the action starts (`lib/client-key.ts`) and go through the outbox's own send, which gives each one retry on a dropped connection or a gateway 5xx and drops the tabs' held reads when the recording lands. An upload's key is the file's own (size, date, a hash of its name; never the name), so the same file picked again is the same recording and opens the one already stored. A tester's match question still asks only about takes made on the recording screen (`isSideKey`), as it did before these had a key. Left alone: Checkout, which already closes older sessions for the customer and keys its Stripe writes. `scripts/check-keys.mjs` reads the wire: 9/9. Ref: #392, #377.

437. 2026-11-11 · **Surfaces take a continuous corner where the browser draws one** · Cards, stages, sheets, tiles, inputs and answer rows are cut with `corner-shape: superellipse(1.5)` in browsers that support it (Chromium today; an iPhone skips the block whole and keeps the round corner). The guide says to add the squircle beside the existing radius; drawn that way every card looked squarer, because a superellipse cuts tighter than the circle it replaces. So each surface radius is drawn a third larger (`--corner-k: 1.35`), which puts the shoulder back where the round one was: the same roundness, a smoother edge (compared at 4x against `squircle` at 1x and 1.84x and `superellipse(1.6)` at 1.5x). Breaks #231's "never a bracketed pixel radius" in spirit only: the theme's radii are restated inside one `@supports` block, and `lib/corners.test.ts` holds the two in step. Pills and Record stay round (a continuous capsule's caps read boxy), and a layer that borrows its surface's radius borrows its corner: the first try left a round layer behind Today's card poking past the continuous one, which is what `scripts/check-corners.mjs` now hunts on every screen in both themes (81/81). Ref: #201, #231, #234.

449. 2026-11-11 · **The tap's tick: haptics on an iPhone** · Timothy, 11 Nov: "Add haptics please", after #448's report named it the change with the most feel left in it. An iPhone gives a page no vibration to ask for, so every `buzz()` was silent on the phone most people hold. The one haptic a page can reach there is the tick iOS plays when a native switch (`<input type="checkbox" switch>`) is thrown, and since iOS 26.5 only a person's own tap may throw it: the hidden-switch-and-`label.click()` trick the report had in mind ("from iOS 18") was closed in May 2026, when WebKit began to require a trusted click. What still works, and what ios-haptics has shipped since its September rewrite (MIT, the technique borrowed, no dependency taken): a transparent `aria-hidden` label laid over a button and wired to a hidden switch, so the real tap lands on the label, reaches the switch as a trusted click, and the phone ticks; the tap bubbles to the button and runs its `onClick` once. `components/ui/TapTick.tsx`, `lib/tap-tick.ts`. It is on the controls that already asked for a haptic at a tap (Record and Stop, Hostile's three, the Shop's "Wear it on You", every topic spin) and on Settings' switches, which tick on a phone because switches do. One fixed tick, at the tap: no patterns, nothing after an `await`, nothing for a moment that is not a tap (the streak's landing, a ring closing stay silent on an iPhone). Drawn only on an iPhone on iOS 18 or later with Haptics on; never on an iPad (no Taptic Engine), on iOS 17 (the switch has no haptic), or where `navigator.vibrate` exists, which keeps `buzz()` and its patterns. Never on a link (a label inside one keeps it from going anywhere) and never on a disabled control; on a bordered button the label fills the button inside its border, so the 1px edge itself is the one place a tap does not tick. Settings' Haptics row is back on an iPhone that can tick (it was taken off in #446 for doing nothing). The overlay is invisible: the recorder is pixel-identical with and without it, both themes. Not felt on a phone here: no browser on this machine has a Taptic Engine, so `scripts/check-haptics.mjs` (39/39) checks everything the tick depends on (the tap reaches the switch, the switch is native, hidden and out of the accessibility tree, the button is pressed exactly once, a keyboard still presses it) and Timothy's iPhone is the test of the tick itself. Ref: #446, #448, #242.

451. 2026-11-11 · **Ethos follows the phone's text size** · Timothy, 11 Nov: "dynamix type alg", read as "all good, do it" (one commit, so one revert if it meant something else). Someone who has turned their iPhone's text up gets every app larger and got Ethos unchanged: its type is in fixed sizes. It follows now, the way the phone's own Display Zoom does: the page is laid out as a narrower phone and shown larger, so the words, the numbers, the marks and the taps grow together, and every layout rule the app has reads the room it really has. A 390pt iPhone one step up lays out at 349 and shows 1.12 times the size; two steps, and everything past them, at 320 and 1.22 times (a 430pt phone reaches 1.34). The layout never goes under the 320 the gates already hold to, so that is the ceiling, well short of the phone's largest sizes. How: the viewport tag's `initial-scale` with no width beside it, written by the head's first script from the size iOS sets body text at (`font: -apple-system-body` on a probe; `lib/text-size.ts`). The app owns the tag: the root layout tells Next to emit none, because Next re-inserts its own on every client navigation and the last tag wins; a `<noscript>` carries the plain one. It is read once as the page loads and never changed on a running page; a text size under the default changes nothing (11px is the floor, #450). Not taken: `-apple-system-body` or a larger rem on the root, as the open queue had it (pixel breakpoints and pixel grids would not follow, so the Log's columns and the tab labels break), and CSS `zoom` (viewport units stop meaning the screen). Settings, Appearance: "Larger text", on, drawn only where the phone's text is set larger and the screen has the room; throwing it saves and loads the page again, the one path that is the same as opening the app. `keyboardUp` (#441) no longer reads the visual viewport's `scale`, which browsers count differently on a page shown larger: how far the page is pinched is the layout's width over the width in view. The recorder is where a narrower layout cost something. A topic runs 52 to 93 characters; at 26px in a 320 layout it stood six lines deep and took Record off the first screen. So the topic at rest is sized to the screen, as Today's is (`min(26px, 6.667vw)`, breaking #331's fixed 26 under 390 wide): the same size on the glass at every text size, while the rest grows. Under 360 wide the rhythm steps in as a game's already did under 700 tall, the card may be as short as its words, a three-across tactic tile steps in 4px top and bottom, and Demos takes 14% of the height while he listens. Two steps up on a 393x852 phone under a Dynamic Island's inset: Record, its caption and the whole screen with nothing to scroll for a day's practice, a lesson and the boss; Record whole for a game; Stop, its ring and Start over while the clock runs. 390 and wider at the default size are pixel-identical; a 375pt phone's topic is 25px and a 360pt one's 24, where Record had been 29px off the screen. Not seen on a phone here: no browser on this machine has a text size, so `scripts/check-text-size.mjs` (91/91) stands the probe's answer in and runs everything else as it is, and `lib/text-size.test.ts` runs the script at every text size on every iPhone and iPad width. What only an iPhone can say: that Safari infers the layout's width from the scale as the specification has it, and whether it hands the page its safe-area insets divided by that scale (the gate assumes not, the tighter case; if not, the gap over the first row is 13pt deeper than at the default). Ref: #450, #441, #446, #331, #412, F12, D51.

455. 2026-11-11 · **The first security audit, and what it closed** · Timothy, 11 Nov: "We've never done a security audit... I want DDoS protection, I want rate limits." Every route, the client, the live database and Vercel were read. No way into another account's data was found. Closed: Next 15.5.29 (a critical advisory); the browser's roles hold only the grants a policy answers and a later table starts with none (0029, applied); every response carries a Content-Security-Policy and the frame, sniff, referrer and permissions headers (`lib/security-headers.ts`; `script-src` keeps 'unsafe-inline' because a nonce would make every static page render per request); a paid take that stores nothing gets its slot back at most six times an hour (#409's give-back kept, its loop closed); Hostile's context is cut to what speech can hold; a push endpoint is only ever a browser maker's push service, eight per account, each send timed out at 8 s; a guest's word lookups count per address; the redeem limiter refuses when it cannot answer; `/rep?back=` goes through `safeNextPath`. Left as they are, on purpose: the tester code's default, the workbench pages in production, and Hostile's verdict judging text the phone holds (its own row only). Owed by hand, Vercel's firewall API being closed to this session: STATE.md, Open.

456. 2026-11-11 · **The audit's second pass: what a guest could still do** · Timothy, the same day: "Could it be better? More secure?" Three things the first pass missed, each found by trying it. A guest session could fill the database: one request wrote 50,000 freeze days and 2 MB values on a local Postgres, the risk 0021 and 0024 had closed for two tables and left open on three (0030, applied). Per-address limits put no end on a script with many addresses, so every guest and visitor now shares one ceiling on paid calls, 300 a day, which asks a guest to create an account and stops no account (`guestall`); it is a number to raise at launch, never to remove. And the `brand` bucket was public with the whole source tree of 10 Aug in it, coach prompt included, no key (0031 closes it; the files are Timothy's to remove, STATE.md Open). Also: a take with no session is scored and never stored; `server-only` on every file that holds a key; `npm audit --omit=dev` at zero (zod 4, set jitless because the browser pass caught it trying eval under the new policy; Next's postcss held by `overrides`) with a weekly workflow to keep it there; the service worker keeps this origin only and no page with a query; IPv6 by its /64; the limiter table swept hourly. Corrected from #455: Supabase's CAPTCHA must stay off until the app sends its token, or every sign-in fails; and the policy now takes `http://supabase.local`, which the first pass would have refused under the look scripts. Left for a later pass, with the design in STATE.md: Hostile's free week held by the server.

457. 2026-11-12 · **A swipe between the tabs: the page under the finger, the app moved only when it lands** · Timothy: "swipe between screens add." Today, Lessons, Practice, Log and You are peers along the bar, so a sideways pull on one brings its neighbour in beside it, the bar's well slides under the same thumb, and a let-go lands all three on one spring at the finger's speed (`components/TabSwipe.tsx`, `lib/tab-swipe.ts`; the apple-design skill §2, §3, §5, §6, §9). It is the lesson walk's swipe, not a second idea of one: pointer events over `touch-action: pan-y`, ten pixels to choose the axis, a quarter of the width or a flick that would carry past half to go, a flick back to cancel, a give past either end of the bar. Three choices are its shape. (1) **The app does not move until the motion lands.** Nothing is navigated while anything is in the air, so a finger can catch a landing and turn it round, and a swipe let go early has changed nothing. (2) **The neighbour is a picture until then** (`lib/tab-stand-in.ts`): the tab as it was last left (the very nodes React let go of) or, for a tab not opened yet, its prerendered first paint, asked for while the phone is idle and in a deployed build only. Mounting the neighbour for real was refused: a tab page reads the address, opens sheets and marks things seen as it mounts (the price drop, a paywall return, a tester's screen count), and a peek must spend none of that. (3) **What already animates a tab change stands down**: a page a swipe carried in plays no push, the well does not travel twice, and under a picture of the tab as it was left the page's own entrances are run to their end before the picture fades (the look loop caught the alternative: a washed frame 240ms after the lift). The bar lights the destination from the lift, as its own slide does, and a thumb that takes the bar's well mid-landing takes only the well: the page still lands (counting that as an interruption stranded the page off-screen, found in review and held by the gate). Left alone: a mouse, the 24px at each screen edge (the system's back gesture), fields, sliders, sideways scrollers, any open sheet, and every screen that is not a tab. Reduced motion: nothing follows the finger and the swipe still changes the tab. The tab bar moved from z-20 to z-40 so the picture (35) passes under it and over a page's own sticky bar (30); sheets stay above at 50. Reference: iOS's paging between peers and Android's pager under a tab row. Checked with real touches against a build: `scripts/check-swipe.mjs` (43/43), `lib/tab-swipe.test.ts`; filmed in `docs/look/swipe/` by `scripts/look-swipe.mjs` (both themes, eight walks).

465. 2026-11-12 · **The swipe goes sooner and holds the finger** · Timothy, 12 Nov: "lower the distance and velocity needed to swipe always hold the screen and disable vertical scroll when swipe is active", after "it'll just glitch back and forth" while held. The rule stays Apple's (#457: where the throw would come to rest, a flick back cancels), with lower lines: 15% of the width dragged goes (was 25%), a throw that would rest past 30% goes (was 50%), and a flick toward the neighbour at 300 px/s goes from any distance (new). Once a swipe has the finger the page's own scroll is refused (a document `touchmove` listener, not passive, cancels while the drag is locked), so a thumb drifting up or down no longer hands the gesture to the scroll halfway. A cancel after that is the browser taking the gesture, not the person changing their mind, so it lands where the finger was instead of snapping home, which is what made the page jump back and forth. The lift counts as the throw's last sample when it moved, since a quick flick arrives as two coalesced moves. Not reproducible here: desktop Chromium already keeps a sideways drag from scrolling, and the old code passes the same held check, so the jumping is iOS Safari's and the fix is checked by Timothy's thumb. `lib/tab-swipe.test.ts`; `scripts/check-swipe.mjs` 49/49 (a held drift that stays under the finger with no scroll, a slow 18% that goes, a quick 50px flick that goes). Ref: #457, #443.

466. 2026-11-12 · **A swipe that moves the page always lands** · Timothy, 12 Nov: "copy Apple's swipe logic... I can have 5 perfect in a row then 5 bad ones", then "it'll move the screen in the direction I want but won't swipe to the next screen, it'll just bounce back". Two causes. A reverse speed of 150 px/s cancelled a swipe from any distance, and a thumb rolling back a few pixels as it lifts reads as that speed, so a full drag bounced home at random. And when the landing slide was cut short (a tap, a re-render), the code read the interruption as a cancel and sent the page home. Now it is Apple's paging rule, decided once at the lift: a flick of 300 px/s or more goes the way it was thrown; anything slower goes when its resting point (where it is plus its momentum) is past 15% of the width on the same side; the slide afterwards only shows that decision and never changes it. `lib/tab-swipe.test.ts` (the roll at 80, 150 and 220 px/s on a 200px drag goes, a short slow pull stays, a real throw back cancels); `scripts/check-swipe.mjs` 50/50, the new check six swipes in a row each ending with an 8px roll back, all landed. Ref: #465, #457.

467. 2026-11-12 · **Coming back to a tab costs nothing it already paid** · Timothy, 12 Nov: "optimise speed since it'll be resource heavy and don't rerender after every swipe back or like Log number animations". Measured before, on a build with the look fixtures, tapping away from a tab and back: every number on Today, the Log and You counted up again from 82%, the Log ran 3 entrances and You 20, and each return read 3 to 12 times (the whole recording spine and every XP event, paged, and the coin and freeze syncs with their writes). Three changes. A number on a tab counts once per visit (`lib/counted.ts`): a return shows it where it landed, a number that moved counts again, and off the bar (a recording's result) it always counts; kept by tab and value, because React's ids are new on every client mount. A tab drawn this visit finishes its entrances before the first paint on a return, and for 900ms after (a MutationObserver) so rows that land a beat later do not fade in either (`components/PageTransition.tsx`, `lib/entrances.ts`); the page's own slide is left alone. And tab to tab, a read or sync held less than a minute is handed back without asking, and two asks for the same rows share one request (`lib/held.ts` `once`); every screen that writes is off the bar, so coming back from one reads fresh, and the existing drops still empty a key the moment a write lands. This replaces #433's "read again behind every arrival" for tab-to-tab moves. The plan (billing) and the session check are still asked every time. After: `scripts/check-tab-return.mjs` 9/9 (before 0/9), `scripts/check-held.mjs` 33/33 with its revisit rule rewritten to this, `scripts/check-swipe.mjs` 50/50 twice, `scripts/check-nav.mjs` 24/24. 475. 2026-11-16 · **The room: the first minute of Ethos is a scripted interview, not a pitch** · docs/plan-next.md section 12; Timothy 12 and 16 Nov. What it is: On a first run, /welcome opens on the room in place of the three pitch screens. A first run means no ?step=, not finished, still on step 0, and no Supabase session in storage (`roomDue`). The questions, the plan and the account ask follow as before, so the account is asked after the scene. The door is the scene behind frosted glass, with one "Hold to step in" and "Not now". Sign in and Tester code stay in the top row. When the hold is full and the finger lifts, the lift is the gesture. Inside it the AudioContext is made, the mic is asked for, and on an iPhone motion is asked for, all before anything is awaited. The scene: a high-rise office at dusk, flat and layered in the spirit of Kurzgesagt. It is our own drawing; nothing of theirs is copied. The interviewer wears a navy suit and has his feet up. He says "Sell me this pen.", you speak, he says "What are you even saying?" and "Why would I need this pen?", you answer, and he says "I'm sorry, but you're not who we're looking for." Demos then rises in on a sheet with "That was alright." or "That was horrible.", then "What if that was real?", then Next. Timothy's calls, as given: No AI on onboarding: no Whisper, no Claude, no server call. Nothing is recorded or sent. No numbers. The lines are scripted and the same for everyone, text only, no voice. They should feel they started rough. The rejection comes whatever was said, which breaks CLAUDE.md's "we never manufacture insecurity" on purpose. Alright or horrible comes from the mic level alone. Only the interview ships. A script is data (`lib/room-script.ts`), so the pitch or small talk can be added later as another object. Claude's calls: The art: Dusk, with the sun low on the right, a tall lit tower on the left that says "high floor", and the pen lying in the sun's reflection on the desk. Nine layers move against the tilt by depth: sky 8px, far city 16, mid 21, near 26, office 30, him 34, his feet and hand 41, desk 48, pen 64. The pen also rolls up to 1.5 degrees. Transform only. Four moods, told apart by pose. His mouth runs three frames while he speaks. The tilt: It is a window: 20 degrees reaches full scale, measured from how the phone was held when the scene began. The centre slowly follows a hand that has been still for 1.2 s. A critically damped spring smooths it every frame. The mouse stands in on a desktop. Reduced motion holds the room still. His words: They stream like an AI chat: about a word every 120 to 180 ms, longer beats at commas and full stops, and a synthesised pop under each chunk. The pop follows the Sound setting and the silent switch, and never plays while the mic is open. Each line is held 0.3 s a word, never less than 1.4 s. The bubble sits over his head, opens at the width of the whole line, and grows upward a line at a time. It is up to 300px wide and slides back over his head when the right edge is near, with its tail kept on him. Type is 20px, and 17px at 320. The integration moved it from beside his head, where at 320 the verdict took five lines and reached into Skip. The hold: Frosted glass that fills with white, the words turning dark where the white has passed. A light runs along the words at rest. The room opens only on the lift. The iPhone tick comes at the lift, because only a click ticks. Your turns: The mic opens only for a turn. A turn ends on 2.5 s of silence after speech, at 25 s, on Done, on the page being hidden, or after 10 s with no voice at all. Alright or horrible is decided against an adaptive noise floor. Horrible if under 5 s voiced in all, under 1.5 s in either turn, over 4 s before the first word, or speech in under 40 percent of the time. The way through: Skip until his last line. Next raises the app's background over the room into the name question. Back from the name returns to the room. If the mic is denied, his lines still play and Demos says "I couldn't hear you, so let's get you set up." (the review's line; the first draft's two fragments broke COPY-RULES) If motion is denied, the room stays still and nothing is said. Permissions: Permissions-Policy now allows the gyroscope and accelerometer for this site; without them Chromium on Android never sends deviceorientation. Reference: plan-next section 12, and Timothy's answers of 16 Nov ("the interviewer no voice. The account asked after it. The art flat laid like Kyrgyzstag... high rise... sell me this pen."). 476. 2026-11-16 · **The Pause dimension reads sentence ends from Whisper's text, and only an um fills a pause, for new takes** · Claude's call, from the pause analysis of the tester cohort's recordings; Timothy may overturn the trace call, which is one line (`MIC_FILLS_PAUSE`). What changed, for new takes only: Whisper's word list carries no punctuation, so a held pause was "before a sentence" only where Whisper happened to start a segment after it, and the six-word finished-point check never found a full stop and counted back up to 41 words; now each segment's punctuated text is lined up with the timed words by their letters (`lib/sentence-ends.ts`), a word ends a sentence when a . ? or ! follows it ("..." and "…" do not, nor a full stop inside a number), and a segment whose text and words disagree keeps the segment-start rule. Filled means an um, uh or erm (the Fillers ring's own set) within 0.5s of either edge of the pause, measured to the end of the um's word (it was any filler, "like" and "you know" included, within 1.2s), and no longer a sound the phone's loudness trace heard in the gap. The opening's 7 points, which a take all but never earned (a pause before the first word is never a gap between two words, so the ceiling was 93), move to placement (67), and filled pauses and dead air cost at most 15 together (uncapped, a longer take paid more for the same habit). Unchanged: beats under 0.8s, dead air past 3.5s, a pause mid-sentence is searching, six words to a finished point, 2 to 9 landings a minute, the last-fifth landing, the Index's weights, and the game round's 1.2s cut-in rule and its own reading of the trace (`lib/round.ts`). The replay (`supabase/replay/pause-replay.sql`: one read-only SELECT, aggregates only, held to the TypeScript word for word and score for score on PGlite by `lib/pause-replay.test.ts`) ran on production on 16 Nov over the cohort's scored recordings. From 6 Nov (after #422's amendment, 67 recordings), #476 without the trace (new_noenv) scored a median 60 with 25% under 40, felt strong +10.1 against the person's own mean and a within-person correlation with felt of +0.23; with the trace read from the stored notes (new) a median 48 with 39% under 40, felt strong -6.3 and -0.18. Over all 108 (2 to 15 Nov): new_noenv a median 56 and +0.31, new a median 20 and -0.10. The mic call: the trace called 16.5% of held pauses voiced, mostly in the recordings people felt were their best, which turned the ranking backwards; until it is shown to hear a swallowed "um" and not a breath or a word's tail, it fills no pause, and a pause is filled only by an um the transcript can point at (feedback traces to a timestamp). `MIC_FILLS_PAUSE` (lib/pause-quality.ts) is false; `judgePauses` and `pauseReport` take `micFills`, defaulting to it, so the trace's path stays alive and tested and comes back with a one-line change; the route and every caller take the default, and the route still passes the trace `pauseEnvelope` admits, which judges nothing. The replay's "new" column is #476 with the trace (micFills true) and "new_noenv" is what ships; neither was renamed. Nothing stored moves (#422, "fix it but dont rescore"): stored kinds, verdicts, Pause scores and Index stay, and every reader of stored verdicts (the Pause row's moments, Hear it back's track, the pause map, the glance, the closing note, the Log) reads them as stored, so a recording whose pause said "with a sound in it. The mic heard it, the transcript didn't." still says it, still names and plays that moment, and still scores it as filled (`lib/stored-scores.test.ts`, `lib/index-score.test.ts`). Side effects for new takes. The Pausing trait counts pauses before a sentence by their kind, and the kinds now come from the full stops: on the real-shaped fixture (`lib/__fixtures__/whisper-take.ts`, 41.38s) it counts 6 where the rules before counted 4 (8.7 a minute against 5.8), while a stored reading stays as it was, so a new take's Pausing reading is not like for like with a stored one. The coach is handed the same kinds: 6 composed and 2 mid-sentence where it was 4 and 4 (8 held either way), and it still calls every pause before a sentence composed whatever its verdict. The trace call moves neither, since both count kinds, not verdicts; but a gap the trace would have called voiced is now a landing when it is one, so the landed count on the glance, the track and the closing note can rise with it. The Index jumps on the first new take against old ones: the delta since last sets it beside a stored take scored under the old rules, and Pause is 150 of the 1000, 1.5 Index points a Pause point; the fixture's same Whisper reply stored Pause 26 (39 points) and scores 73 now (110), +71 on the Index from the rule alone, so the first new take can read well up on its delta and as a best against stored takes. That is the rule, not the speaker, and it is expected, as #422's delta was; the delta is left as it is. Ref: #120, #121, #422, #424, #427. Review, 16 Nov: with the trace off, five route tests and the route's proof that the Index scores the stored verdicts passed whatever the route did; `lib/analyze-route.test.ts` now judges those with the trace switched back on (`traceOn`), and the replay holds the um's 0.5s line from both sides (an um 0.52s from a pause fills neither; the SQL drawn at 0.49 to 1.2 fails). Merged beside #471's out-of-100 rows: the stored-before-#476 test reads the Log's Pause row as the stored 26.

481. 2026-11-16 · **365 topics, one a day, and every trait walks its whole tier** · Timothy, 16 Nov: "more prompts as well. add 365 please." t61 to t365 (`lib/topics.ts`) are the 305 the judge kept from 350 written across five themes (ambition, choices, ideas, people, the world): 45 were cut as duplicates, near-duplicates, too vague, or for money ("paid"), and 24 were rewritten. 498. 2026-11-18 · **Trait levels fill with trait XP: every scored recording pays each trait its score** · Timothy, 18 Nov: "xp that fills with practice." On his live call this reverses #482's closest-try bar, and the renumbering #482 rejected, and it replaces #159's best-row rule (a take's best row levels its trait at 70, twice from 90). Every scored recording pays each trait it read that trait's score, rounded and held to 0 to 100, as trait XP (`lib/traits.ts` `repTraitXp`). The substance gate is still re-run on the stored transcript, so a take too short to score pays nothing. A trait read at 0 gets 0 and a trait not read is left out, so attendance alone never levels anything and the levels stay earned. I chose the score over bands because "+45" then matches the 45 on the recording's row. Level n costs 70 × n (`LEVEL_STEP`; totals 70, 210, 420, 700, 1050, 1470): level 1 is one Strong read, and each later level costs 70 more, a few more strong recordings each time. The step was chosen from 400 synthetic accounts drawn from production's percentiles (42 accounts; recordings per account median 4.5, p90 14; tier-2 assumed at 40/60/75/85, account skill correlation 0.5). The median first recording takes two of the five taught traits to level 1, and 88% level at least one. At 5 recordings the best trait is at level 2 to 3 (p50 2, p75 3); at 14, level 5; at 30, level 7 (p75 8). `lib/traits.test.ts` holds the table. A recording pays at most 100 and every level after the first costs 140 or more, so one take crosses at most one level per trait. Levels are still derived and never stored, with no migration: **every stored account renumbers on its next load** (old Filler control 12 with Pausing 0 becomes something like 4/4/4/3/3), and no level-down moment can show, because the moment compares before and after under the same new rule and the old rule exists nowhere. `traitLevels` returns each trait's level, total XP, XP into the level, the next level's cost, the bar's percent (rounded down, 0 to 99) and what the last recording paid; You ranks by XP. You's rows keep LevelMeter, "Level 6" and "Not yet", and the row's screen-reader line is unchanged because it still describes the bar. The sheet's line is "331 XP to level 7. Last recording +45." with "Each recording adds its Filler control score as XP." under it. It says the XP still to go, as #485 found "40 of 50 to level 2" failed the two-second test. The result's moment: `levelUps` compares the history before the take with that same history plus the stored row, so a failed save crosses nothing. It names only traits You shows (a free account's five), biggest first, at most two, the rest counted: "Pace reached level 3, Variety level 3 and 3 more", each trait behind its 8px colour mark (DESIGN rule 6). It sits in the glance's earned line under a game's ladder and above "Best yet". The glance is the only place a level-up is said, while a best keeps its card in the sheet and Demos's pose. Under #427's budget the line is usually cut to its first trait, and it can push Demos's next-time cue off the glance; the cue is still carried to the next day. A recording that crossed nothing shows nothing new. Two XPs, told apart in two seconds: trait XP is never added up and only appears inside its trait (the sheet under the trait's name and sage meter, a rule line naming the trait), and the moment says "level", never "XP". Account XP (#485, purple, hexagon, 10 a recording) is the only XP with a total. Removed: `LEVEL_AT`, `DOUBLE_AT`, `traitGain`, `repTraitGain`, `needToLevel`, `levelPercent`, `closestLine`, with their tests; `STRONG_AT` is a plain 70 of its own. Fixtures: `LEVEL_MIX` is 14 recordings in every state; `look-levels.mjs` and `look-levels-edges.mjs` are on the new rule. Camera: `scripts/look-trait-xp.mjs`; shots and sheets in `docs/look/trait-xp/`. Gates: check-apple 28/28, check-type 24/24, check-colour 42/42, check-text-size 91/91, check-a11y 15/15, check-sheet-touch 66/66, check-gaps 70/70. check-motion fails at its first step on the base too, because /welcome opens on the room since #497. Review (18 Nov): a level-up gives up its words before Demos's cue does (its forms run down to "Pace: level 3"), and the first recording's glance holds its 28 words with a level-up, keeping the form that fits. The camera prints the cue and the visible words beside the earned line. Shots: docs/look/trait-xp/review/. Merged with #505: a trait's level-up plays the level sound (`level: !!ladder?.moment || !!levelUp`), swapping into the waiting top slot when it lands after the stored row. Integration, 18 Nov: a road take now ends on "Done for today" (#493), one word shorter, which let a carried cue's short forms (#477) fit beside "Pace reached level 3", so the glance dropped the moment chip and "Tomorrow:" while the level-up kept its long form, the reverse of #498's review order. fitGlance's short-form path now tries every level-up form inside each line and cue form, so "Pace: level 3" comes first (6a5231f). Gates left stale by other branches were brought to the code they watch without weakening them: check-motion and check-motion-layer (#500's Today), check-gaps and check-corners (#501's walk), check-motion's 380ms streak roll (#505), check-room's guest door after the test (#496); .card-score and .intro-dot removed as dead. Merge review, 18 Nov: once no cue form fitted, fitGlance kept the line's long form beside the level-up's shortest, so a road take with a long cue answer went one or two words over its ceiling; now the short line is tried before anything passes the ceiling, and where nothing fits the glance takes the fewest words (a59a7ad). lib/glance.test.ts holds the worst case as a property over 256 combinations. Also: .tone-wash (left by the deleted FillerHeatmap) is gone and lib/css-readers.test.ts fails any class with no reader beyond the eleven that predate the merge; the stored page's dead `name === "Boss"` check is gone; STATE.md corrected in seven places. Left open: Hostile's take card (WPM, and held pauses counted in sage), and account XP still paid and ticked on You for a take #504 gives no star.

503. 2026-11-18 · **The lesson icons are bold 3D objects in the stat icons' family, and Hostile and the boss get marks of their own** · Timothy, 18 Nov: "Let's use Higgsfield to change the icon colors. I mean the lesson things... Let's just use Higgsfield right now." He also said that Hostile Q&A and the cold topic on the homepage "doesn't look that good". The fifteen pale Liquid Glass objects (#322) are re-rendered in the stat icons' style (#487). Each lesson keeps its object (gearstick, zipper, checkered flag, pause bars and a dot, target, tag, gauge, S-arrow, road, boomerang, cube, door, magnifier bubble, orb in a bowl, hourglass). Each is now chunky and glossy in its unit's colour made bold: Pausing cobalt #2F7BFF, Fillers raspberry #F2386F, Restarts honey amber #F2A20B, Pace emerald #16B86A, Variety violet #7C4DFF. Variety is bluer than XP's purple bolt and Pausing is deeper than the freeze's sky, so no unit reads as a stat. The renders are Higgsfield GPT Image 2.6 at high quality, 1:1, on a transparent ground, with the stat flame and cup masters as references so the two sets are one family. Two lessons were rendered first and checked beside the stat icons at 24 to 96px on #121212 and white. Re-renders: the road (it came back on a grey ground), the target (amber, to match the flag and the arrow, not the streak's orange) and the boomerang (it read as a checkmark, then as a caret; the kept one has a return arc). That is 23 renders and 34.5 credits; the job ids are in the commits. `scripts/cut-lesson-icons.mjs` rebuilds `public/lessons/*.webp` (360) and `large/*.webp` (560) at their old paths and writes `lib/lesson-art-bounds.ts`. It floors the renderer's haze (higher for the boomerang's trail), trims, and evens the optical size. The objects now fill about the share of the square the glass ones did with their shadows. The three glass-art scripts are deleted so a FORCE run can never pull the glass set back. Two new marks join StatIcon, the one home for the 3D files, because a second component would be the same img again: Hostile Q&A is a coral bubble struck by a yellow bolt (picked over a cracked bubble, which vanished at 24px; not a shield, which reads as security). The weekly boss is a sky mountain with a red pennant, the week's big climb; it is not a crown, flame or cup, which the set already uses. The two marks stand at 24px beside the names on Today's two tiles, where the line shield and stopwatch were. They are marks at a name's side, as the streak chip's flame is, so #478's "only the card stands an object" holds. Practice's Hostile tile wears the bubble at 28px. The 3D bubble beside Q&A's line glyph breaks the tile row's one grammar on purpose: Hostile has no ladder and is the same door as on Today. The boss gets no mark on Practice, because Demos holding his clock is already that stage's figure. This undoes the fix round's "the colour left is the object's own pastel" (#391), on Timothy's live call. Unlike that round's tinted squares, the colour is in the object, on the one neutral tile every row shares, as #359 and #487 put it. On Today's card each object is lighter and more saturated than its own family's card. Tested in all five families in both themes, the existing drop shadow separates them, so no edge or glow was added. The service worker is on v20 and precaches the two marks. `lib/lesson-icons.test.ts` covers the fifteen files, their sources, the bounds and the precache. 504. 2026-11-18 · **Stars are three goals you can see: a real answer, under 11 slips a minute, under 3** · Timothy, 18 Nov: "1 stars and 1 stars, these people gonna be discouraged ... 2 star should be easy ... in every game, the stars are always measured ... there's too much text". Production's last 30 days (213 takes, 26 accounts) ran 60% one star, 23% two, 16% three under the old cut (under 3 disfluencies a minute for three, under 6 for two, substance capping both). Each star is now one measured goal (#10), each harder than the one before and each needing it (lib/metrics.ts `starsFor`): star 1 is a real answer (`isScorable`: 20+ words, not one phrase on repeat), star 2 fewer slips (fillers plus restarts) than 11 a minute, star 3 fewer than 3. The rate is held as a count for the take's own length (`slipLimit`, ceil of rate × minutes, never under 1), so the glance draws each goal under its star in one to three words ("20+ words", "Under 12 slips", "Under 4 slips" at 62s, "No slips" when none are allowed), sage when met, grey when not, its words turning sage as its star lands on its own empty slot (components/StarGoals.tsx). The goals ride on the glance's word ceiling (GLANCE_WORDS_STARS, 8), and no other star text is left: "1 star for showing up" and "1 star, nothing measured" went, because a take too thin to score now earns 0, not 1 for participation. Length goals were ruled out because Speed rush's 20s level could never reach two stars. Restarts have no column, so the cut points rest on a model fitted to the old split and the filler-rate and length percentiles, which lib/stars.test.ts holds: expected 0/1/2/3 stars on 6/28/46/19% of takes, star 2 on 70% of scored takes (64 to 77% across fits) and star 3 on 20% (19 to 24%). Substance no longer caps a star it does not name. Stored stars stay (#422: restarts are not stored, so nothing can re-derive them exactly). Migration 0035 widens the column's check to 0 to 3, and until it is applied saveRep stores a 0 at 1. The road moves on at one star, then asks for every second star before any third (`nextLesson` [1, 2, 3]); its unit gates keep their numbers and open about a lesson sooner, and the walk still takes units in order. Games still count a round on two stars, now about 70% of takes instead of about 40%. #504 review: My own refit (434 restart models that reproduce the old 16/23 split) gives star 2 on 60-79% of scored takes (p10-p90 64-75%, median 70%) and star 3 on 16-27%. Star 3 is exactly the old under-3-a-minute line without the substance cap, and every edge case breaks cleanly. The LOOK_BEST fixture lit 'Under 4 slips' on 5 slips; it is fixed, and the glance camera now fails any lit goal its take does not meet. #505 review: Stacks of sounds clipped through Chromium's limiter (+0.6 and +1.6 dBFS). A WaveShaper soft ceiling now ends the master and stacks peak at -0.5 or lower, checked by the audition script. A dropped cue is now silenced whole, every tap wakes the context until it runs, and closeSound closes it. Hostile's reduced-motion sound moved to a timer so StrictMode plays it once. The intro patch is rebuilt on wt-onboard cd39f4a and the wt-xp merge note corrected (the only conflict is the imports in app/rep/page.tsx, plus `level: !!ladder?.moment || !!levelUp`).

505. 2026-11-18 · **Every win has its own sound, sized to the win: a ding per star, a payout on three, then the take's biggest moment** · Timothy, 18 Nov: "play these sounds, but give them the dopamine ... you completed a lesson ... Casino, bang. Blackjack 21 hand ... oh wow, your big payout ... make it addictive". This amends #138 and #140: "one sound per take" goes, and the arousal of a real win is turned up; the line #140 kept does not move, since a sound means something was earned, so a take with no star is silent and nothing plays on a loss (Dixon et al.'s measured harm is the jingle on a loss). One kit: lib/sound.ts (one AudioContext made only in a tap, the Sound switch, the ambient audio session so the silent switch holds and music keeps playing, a master with a generated room, a 140 Hz high-pass, a 6 kHz shelf and a limiter at -3 dBFS) and lib/sound-recipes.ts (every sound as notes in D major, the streak chime's key, in instrument voices built from partials, the coin scatter on a fixed seed). lib/sfx.ts is gone. The glance plays a ding as each star lands (star 1 a warm mallet D5, which is the one-star sound; star 2 a bell F#5; star 3 glass A5 with coins), the payout once three have (a bright D major chord, 16 coins, a last ka-ching), then one moment, the rarest first: the boss (a G to D plagal cadence), a level, a lesson (ta-ta-daaa), a best, a round. The streak's moment has the flame catching, the chime and the odometer's clicks, its count rolling on the landing note (`STREAK_LAND_S`). XP today on You ticks as it climbs, only when it rose since this phone last heard it. A Hostile claim held at 70 or more wins its round. Reduced motion keeps every sound; the switch silences it. The Index count stays silent because a score can fall. Cues are laid on the audio clock in a layout effect, early by the output latency: a passive effect on a busy glance ran 230 to 300ms behind the stars (LOOK_SOUND=1 measured it). Nobody here can hear, so the sounds are checked by construction and by render: scripts/sound-audition.mjs renders each sound and each moment through the kit's own chain in Chromium's offline audio, with every true peak at -1.7 dBFS or under and loudness rising with the win (one star -19.3 LUFS, three stars -14.1). Timothy hears them at /workbench/sound and docs/sound-audition/index.html. Settings' Sound reads "For stars, lessons and your streak." and plays the one-star sound when turned on. I synthesised rather than generating clips (Higgsfield): no files, nothing on the first paint, works offline, the same every time.

The audit (docs/speed/audit.md) measured the baseline at 54204ca on a phone-like link (150 ms round trip, 1.6 Mbps down, 750 kbps up, CPU slowed 4x). The server heard a 60 s take 10.3 s after Stop (897 KB at 128 kbps). Today's first open took 5.5 s to draw, with 569 KB of JS and the worker's 1.27 MB install beside it; a later open took 1.6 s. You's first open from Today took 649 ms. The room's first frame came at 3.7 s, or 4.7 s from the home-screen icon, behind an empty server page. Also one structured stage line per take in /api/analyze, with nothing personal in it. Before (99de507) and after, same harness: the wait after Stop went from 10.3 s to 2.3 s. The room's door is painted at 1.1 s instead of 3.7 s (1.5 s from the icon instead of 4.5 s), but it answers a hold only once React has hydrated, at about 3.1 s (3.5 to 3.9 s from the icon), so the usable gain is about 0.6 s. Today's first open went from 5.2 s to 4.7 s (LCP 6.3 to 5.2), and the day's first open from 6.1 to 4.6 s. On the installed app's first open the splash was gone at 3.7 s instead of 4.3 s, with Today drawn at 4.8 s instead of 5.4 s. You from Today went from 642 to 165 ms. The recorder went from 4.2 to 3.4 s, the Log from 4.8 to 3.9 s, Practice from 4.4 to 3.7 s. The JS every page carries went from 226 to 202 KB gz, and Today's route from 419 to 310 KB gz. The calls before Whisper went from three in a row to two round trips. A later open's splash holds 0.75 s longer and lifts onto the drawn Today, not its skeleton: no skeleton after the brand moment, and no streak ticking once it is gone.
